## Dedication / 献词

===Original===
For 

 my sons, 

 Jimmy and Bill 

 eight and seven 

 who 

 prefer King Kong
===Chinese===
献给

 我的儿子们，

 吉米和比尔

 一个八岁，一个七岁，

 他们

 更爱《金刚》

## Epigraph / 题词

===Original===
> The first law of every being, is to preserve itself and live. You sow hemlock, and expect to see ears of corn ripen
===Chinese===
> 一切存在的第一法则，便是保全自身、活下去。你播下毒芹的种子，却指望看见麦穗成熟

## Part I / 第一部

### I / 一

===Original===
Sam Vettori sat staring into Halsted Street. He was a big man, fat as a hog, with a dark, oily complexion, kinky black hair and a fat, aquiline face. In repose he had an air of lethargic good-nature, due entirely to his bulk; for in reality he was sullen, bad-tempered and cunning. From time to time he dragged out a huge gold watch and looked at it with raised eyebrows and pursed lips.
Near him at a round table sat Otero, called The Greek, Tony Passa, and Sam Vettori’s lieutenant, Rico, playing stud for small stakes. Under the green-shaded lamp Otero’s dark face looked livid and cavernous. He sat immobile and said nothing, win or lose. Tony, robust and rosy, scarcely twenty years old, watched each turn of the cards intently, shouting with joy when his luck was good, cursing when it was bad, more out of excitement than interest in the stakes. Rico sat with his hat tilted over his eyes, his pale, thin face slightly drawn, his fingers tapping. Rico always played to win.
Vettori, puffing, pulled himself to his feet and began to walk up and down.
“Where you suppose he is?” he asked the ceiling. “I told him eight o’clock. It is half-past.”
“Joe never knows what time it is,” said Tony.
“Joe’s no good,” said Rico without taking his eyes off the cards, “he’s soft.”
===Chinese===
萨姆·维托里坐着，凝望哈尔斯特德街。他是个大块头，肥得像头猪，肤色黝黑油腻，一头卷曲的黑发，一张肥大的鹰钩脸。不动声色时，他有种懒洋洋的好脾气模样，那全然是块头撑出来的；实际上他阴沉、暴躁、又狡诈。他不时掏出一只硕大的金表，眉毛扬起，嘴唇噘着看表。
他旁边一张圆桌边，坐着人称「希腊人」（The Greek）的奥特罗（Otero）、托尼·帕萨（Tony Passa），还有萨姆·维托里的副手里科，三人在玩小注梭哈。绿灯罩的台灯下，奥特罗的黑脸显得铁青而深陷。他坐着一动不动，输赢都不吭声。托尼壮实红润，年方二十不到，每一轮翻牌都盯得入神，手气好时欢呼大叫，手气坏时咒骂不休，与其说是心疼赌注，不如说是兴奋使然。里科坐着，帽檐压到眼睛上，苍白瘦削的脸微微绷紧，手指敲着桌面。里科打牌从来只为赢。
维托里喘着粗气，撑着站起来，开始来回踱步。
「你猜他人在哪儿？」他对着天花板发问。「我约的八点。现在都半点了。」
「乔从来不知道几点钟。」托尼说。
「乔不顶用，」里科说，眼睛不离牌，「他太软。」

===Original===
“Well,” said Vettori, stopping to watch the game out of boredom, “maybe so. But we can’t do without him, Rico. I tell you, Rico, he can go anywhere. A front is what he’s got. Swell hotels? What does it mean to that boy? He says to the clerk, ‘I would like please a suite.’ A suite! You see, Rico. We can’t do without him.”
Rico tapped on the table, flushing slightly.
“All right, Sam,” he said, “some day he’ll turn yellow. Hear what I say. He’s not right. What’s all this dancing? A man don’t dance for money.”
Sam laughed.
“Oh, Rico! You don’t know Joe.”
Tony stared at Rico.
===Chinese===
「呃，」维托里说，闲得无聊便停下来看牌，「也许吧。可我们离不了他，里科。我跟你说，里科，他去得了任何地方。他有的是一副门面。高级酒店？对那小子算个啥？他对柜台说：『劳驾，我要一间套房。』套房！你明白了吧，里科。我们离不了他。」
里科敲了敲桌子，脸微微涨红。
「行吧，萨姆，」他说，「早晚有一天他会怂的。记住我的话。他不对劲。跳个什么舞？男人不该靠跳舞挣钱。」
萨姆笑了。
「哎呀，里科！你不了解乔。」
托尼盯着里科。

===Original===
“Rico,” he said, “Joe’s right. I know what I’m saying. All that dancing is a front. He’s smart. Have they ever got him?”
Rico slammed down his cards. He hated Joe and he knew that Tony and Vettori knew it.
“All right,” he said, “hear what I say. He’ll turn yellow some day. A man don’t take money for dancing.”
“I win,” said Otero.
Rico pushed the money towards him and got to his feet.
“Well, if he don’t show up in ten minutes I’ll take the air,” said Rico.
===Chinese===
「里科，」他说，「乔没问题。我知道自己在说什么。跳舞那一套全是门面。他精着呢。有谁抓到过他吗？」
里科把牌摔在桌上。他恨乔，而且他知道托尼和维托里都清楚这一点。
「行，」他说，「记住我的话。早晚有一天他会怂。男人不该靠跳舞收钱。」
「我赢了。」奥特罗说。
里科把钱推给他，站起身来。
「行，他要是十分钟内还不露面，我就出去透风。」里科说。

===Original===
“You stay where you are,” said Vettori, his face hardening.
Tony watched the two of them intently. Otero counted his money. One day, Vettori had said to Rico, “You are getting too big for us.” Tony remembered the look he had seen in Rico’s eyes. Lately they had all been talking about it. Rico was getting too big for them. Scabby, the informer, said: “Tony, mark what I say. It’s Rico or Sam. One or the other.”
“I’ll stay ten minutes,” said Rico.
Vettori sat down by the window and stared into Halsted Street.
“Two-fifty,” said Otero.
“I’ll match you for it,” said Tony.
===Chinese===
「你给我待在原地。」维托里说，脸色沉了下来。
托尼紧盯着他们俩。奥特罗数着他的钱。有一天，维托里曾对里科说：「你在我们这儿翅膀太硬了。」托尼记得当时他看见里科眼里的那副神情。近来他们都在议论这事。里科在他们中间翅膀越来越硬。线人斯卡比（Scabby）说过：「托尼，记住我的话。不是里科，就是萨姆。总得倒下一个。」
「我待十分钟。」里科说。
维托里在窗边坐下，凝望哈尔斯特德街。
「两块五。」奥特罗说。
「我跟你掷硬币赌它。」托尼说。

===Original===
“No,” said Otero.
Joe Massara opened the door and came in.
“Well,” said Vettori, “you call this eight o’clock?”
Joe got out of a big ulster. He was in evening clothes. His black hair was sleek and parted in the middle. He was vain of his resemblance to the late Mr. Rudolph Valentino.
“Sorry,” said Joe, “the bridge was up. Well, what’s the dirt?”
“Draw up a chair,” said Vettori, “all of you.”
===Chinese===
「不干。」奥特罗说。
乔·马萨拉推门进来。
「好啊，」维托里说，「你管这叫八点钟？」
乔脱下一件宽大的粗呢大衣。他一身晚礼服。黑头发油光水滑，从中间分开梳缝。他颇以自己长得像已故的鲁道夫·瓦伦蒂诺（Rudolph Valentino）先生而自得。
「抱歉，」乔说，「桥吊起来了。怎么样，有什么门道？」
「搬椅子过来，」维托里说，「你们全都过来。」

===Original===
They grouped themselves around the table under the green-shaded lamp. Joe put his hands on the table so they could see his well-manicured nails and the diamond ring the dancer, Olga Stassoff, had given him.
“Now,” said Vettori, “I’ll do the talking. I know what I got to say and you birds keep quiet till I’m through﻿ ﻿…”
“How long will it take?” asked Joe, smiling.
“Shut up and listen,” said Rico.
“All right, all right,” said Vettori, patting them both, “no bad blood. Now: ever hear of the Casa Alvarado?”
“Sure,” said Joe, “it’s an up and up place. One of Francis Wood’s joints. I nearly got an engagement there once.”
===Chinese===
他们在绿灯罩台灯下围桌坐定。乔把双手摆在桌上，好让人看见他修剪齐整的指甲，还有舞女奥尔加·斯塔索夫（Olga Stassoff）送他的钻石戒指。
「好了，」维托里说，「我来讲话。我知道自己要说什么，你们这帮鸟人给我闭嘴，等我讲完……」
「得讲多久？」乔笑着问。
「闭嘴，听着。」里科说。
「行了行了，」维托里说，拍了拍他们俩，「别伤和气。听着：听说过卡萨·阿尔瓦拉多（Casa Alvarado）吗？」
「当然，」乔说，「那是个规规矩矩的场子。弗朗西斯·伍德（Francis Wood）的买卖之一。有回我差点儿在那儿签下演出合同。」

===Original===
Rico spread out his hands.
“See? They know him. He won’t do.”
“No, they never seen me. It was all done through an agent.”
“All right,” said Vettori, “that’s the place.”
Joe looked startled. Rico smiled and taking off his hat began to comb his hair with a little ivory pocket comb.
“It’d be tough,” said Joe, “what’s in it?”
===Chinese===
里科摊开双手。
「瞧见没？他们认识他。他不行。」
「不，他们从没见过我。全是通过经纪人办的。」
「好，」维托里说，「就那家了。」
乔显得吃了一惊。里科微微一笑，摘下帽子，用一把小象牙随身梳梳起头发来。
「那可不好干，」乔说，「里头有多少油水？」

===Original===
“Plenty,” said Vettori. “They only bank once or twice a week. They’re careless, get that; because they’ve never been tapped. It’s easy.”
Joe took out a gold cigarette case which he handled with ostentation.
“Well? I’m listening.”
Vettori refused a cigarette and pulled out a stogie. Downstairs a jazz band began to play and a saxophone sent vibrations along the floor.
“Nine o’clock,” said Otero.
Vettori lit his stogie.
===Chinese===
「多得很。」维托里说。「他们一星期才往银行存一两回款。他们松懈，记住这点；因为从没人动过他们。容易。」
乔掏出一只金烟盒，故意摆弄得人人都看得见。
「怎么样？我听着呢。」
维托里没接烟，掏出一支廉价雪茄。楼下爵士乐队开了场，一支萨克斯管把震颤顺着地板送上来。
「九点了。」奥特罗说。
维托里点着雪茄。

===Original===
“They got a safe,” he said, “that a baby could crack. Too easy to talk about. But that’s on the side. What we’re after is the cashier. The place is lousy with jack. I got the lowdown from Scabby. Well, what do you say, Joe?”
“Yeah,” Rico cut in, “take it or leave it. We ain’t begging you.” Vettori’s face hardened but he said nothing.
“If you say it’s good,” said Joe, “it’s good with me.”
“All right, all right,” said Vettori. “Now, you Tony; we want a big car. Get that. A big, fast car. Get one when I tell you. Steve’s got the plates all ready. Yeah?”
“I’m on, Sam.”
Tony pulled out a cigarette and lit it with a flourish, but his hands shook a little.
===Chinese===
「他们有个保险柜，」他说，「娃娃都能撬开。容易得都不值一提。但那是捎带的。我们要的是出纳。那场子里钱多得发臭。底细是斯卡比给我摸来的。怎么样，乔，你怎么说？」
「就是，」里科插嘴，「爱干不干。我们可不求你。」维托里的脸沉下来，但他什么也没说。
「你说这活儿好，」乔说，「那就是好。」
「好，好。」维托里说。「现在说你，托尼；我们要一辆大车。听清了。一辆又大又快的车。等我的话头去弄。史蒂夫（Steve）把车牌都备好了。明白吗？」
「明白，萨姆。」
托尼抽出一支烟，潇洒地点上，手却有点抖。

===Original===
“Rico and Otero,” said Vettori, looking at each in turn, “will handle the rods. Yeah?”
Rico said nothing. But Otero smiled, showing his stained teeth, and said:
“That’s us, eh, Rico?”
“Well,” Vettori went on. “I guess we got that over. Now, Joe, I want you on the inside. Dress yourself up like you are, see, and fix it so you’ll get there at midnight. All the whistles’ll be tooting and everybody’ll be drunk and won’t know nothing. See? Now you get there at midnight and go to the cigar counter for change. At twelve-five the fun’ll begin. We’ll set our watches by telephone, because I don’t want you here that night. All right. Rico and Otero come in quick, maybe Tony, too, if you can get a good safe place to park. That’s up to Rico. He’s bossing the job.”
Rico looked at Joe.
“Now, they’ll stick you up if everything’s OK. If not, give them the high sign and they’ll beat it. We ain’t taking no chances, because one night don’t make much difference, only New Year’s Eve’s a good night, see? All right. You play like you don’t know them, got it? But while they’re working, you got your eyes open, see? And if something happens, you got a rod, but don’t use it. We got to watch that.”
===Chinese===
「里科和奥特罗，」维托里说，挨个看了他们一眼，「管枪。怎么样？」
里科不吭声。奥特罗却笑了，露出烟熏的黄牙，说：
「那就是咱们了，嗯，里科？」
「好，」维托里接着说。「这一节算是过去了。现在，乔，我要你在里头接应。就照你这身打扮穿出去，明白吧，安排好半夜十二点到场。到时候哨子全在响，人人都喝得烂醉，什么都不知道。懂吗？你十二点到那儿，去雪茄柜台换零钱。十二点零五分，好戏开场。咱们电话对表，因为那天晚上我不要你来这儿。好。里科和奥特罗快速进场，托尼说不定也去，要是能找个又稳妥又保险的停车处的话。那就看里科的了。这活儿他当家。」
里科看着乔。
「听好，一切顺利的话，他们会拿枪顶住你。若不顺，你给他们打个暗号，他们就撤。我们不冒任何险，因为差一晚没什么要紧，只是除夕夜是个好日子，明白吧？好。你装作不认识他们，懂吗？但他们动手的时候，你把眼睛睁大，明白？真出了事，你身上有枪，但别用。这点我们得防着。」

===Original===
Vettori shifted his stogie and shook his finger sideways at Rico.
“That’s your trouble, Rico. The Big Boy can’t fix murder. He can fix anything but murder. Get that. You’re too quick with the lead. If that guy over at the pool room’d died we wouldn’t none of us be sitting here right now.﻿ ﻿…”
Otero broke in vehemently, surprising them.
“But he had to! He had to! Rico does what is right.”
“All right,” said Vettori, “but take it easy. Now, Joe, you got your hands up, but you watch. If nothing happens nobody knows the difference. But if something does happen, you pull the rod and help the boys get out. All right. Here’s the dope. Get what’s in the cash register first. Get that because that’s easy. If things go right, tackle the safe; it’ll probably be open. Another thing: no frisking in the lobby. That’s too dangerous and takes too long. Let the yaps keep their money. All right.”
===Chinese===
维托里换了换咬着的雪茄，手指朝里科横向点了点。
「那就是你的毛病，里科。大人物（Big Boy）摆不平谋杀。除了谋杀他什么都能摆平。记住。你动铅弹动得太快。台球房那边那家伙要是死了，咱们这会儿一个也别想坐在这儿……」
奥特罗激烈地插进来，让他们吃了一惊。
「可他那是不得不！不得不！里科做的事没有不对的。」
「行了，」维托里说，「不过悠着点。现在，乔，你双手举起来，可你眼睛盯着。没出事，谁也看不出分别。真出了事，你就拔枪，帮弟兄们脱身。好。听仔细了。先拿收银机里的钱。先拿那个，因为那容易。顺手了再动保险柜；多半开着。还有一条：别在大堂里搜身。那太险，也太费工夫。让那帮土包子留着他们的钱。行了。」

===Original===
Vettori took a map from his pocket and spread it out on the table. The men crowded round him.
“You go straight in,” said Vettori, marking the route with a pencil, “on the right is the checkroom; watch the girls behind the counter, Joe. On the left is the cigar counter and the cashier’s desk. At the end of the lobby is a big door; the real joint’s beyond that. If things go right, nobody in the place’ll know it’s been stuck up, except maybe some yaps in the lobby. Get the idea? With all them horns tooting and all that damn noise, see? All right. On the right of the lobby is a door and that goes into the manager’s office. The box is in there. The manager’s a goddamn bohunk and there ain’t an ounce of fight in him. See? Scabby give me the lowdown.”
Vettori rolled up his map, put it in his pocket, then looked at his watch.
“Well,” he said, “got it all?”
Joe turned his diamond ring round on his finger and looked at the table.
“What’s the word, Joe?” said Rico.
===Chinese===
维托里从口袋里掏出一张地图，摊在桌上。众人围拢过来。
「你们直着往里走，」维托里说，用铅笔标出路线，「右手是衣帽间；盯住柜台后面的姑娘们，乔。左手是雪茄柜台和出纳台。大堂尽头是一扇大门；真正的场子在那后头。事情顺了，场子里没人会知道被抢了，顶多大堂里几个土包子晓得。明白这意思吧？满街喇叭呜哇乱响，一片该死的吵闹，懂吗？好。大堂右手有扇门，通经理办公室。保险柜就在里头。那经理是个该死的东欧佬，身上没有一两打架的骨头。明白？斯卡比给我摸的底。」
维托里卷起地图揣回口袋，然后看了看表。
「怎么样，」他说，「都记下了？」
乔用手指转着钻戒，眼睛看着桌子。
「你怎么说，乔？」里科说。

===Original===
“It’s a tough one, Rico. What’s the guarantee?”
“Guarantee, hell!” cried Rico. “Why, a blind guy could do your stand.”
“Well, I ain’t doing no time for fifty bucks,” said Joe.
Vettori laughed.
“I’ll give you a couple hundred now,” he said.
Joe nodded.
===Chinese===
「这活儿凶险，里科。有什么担保？」
「担保，见鬼！」里科嚷道。「得了吧，瞎子都能干你那摊活儿。」
「行啊，五十块钱可不够我蹲号子的。」乔说。
维托里笑了。
「我现在就给你两百。」他说。
乔点点头。

===Original===
“All right. I’m in. Never mind the couple hundred.”
They all got to their feet. Below them the jazz band was still playing and the saxophone was still sending vibrations along the floor.
“What’ll you have, boys,” said Vettori, “want some drinks sent up?”
“Not me,” said Tony, “I’m going over and see my woman.”
Otero clapped his hands.
“He’s got a woman.”
===Chinese===
「行。我入伙。那两百就免了。」
众人都站起身。楼下爵士乐队还在演奏，萨克斯管还在把震颤顺着地板送上来。
「喝点什么，弟兄们，」维托里说，「要叫人送酒上来吗？」
「我不喝，」托尼说，「我要过去看我的女人。」
奥特罗拍起手来。
「他有女人了。」

===Original===
Rico hit Otero on the back.
“The Greek’s got a woman too,” said Rico.
Otero with his hands cupped made a series of curves in the air. Joe was patronizing; Olga Stassoff, the dancer, was his woman.
“A beauty is she, Otero?”
“*Si, señor.*”
“Well,” said Vettori, “want any drinks sent up?”
===Chinese===
里科拍了拍奥特罗的后背。
「希腊人也有个女人。」里科说。
奥特罗双手拢成杯形，在空中画出一路曲线。乔一副居高临下的架势；舞女奥尔加·斯塔索夫才是他的女人。
「是个美人儿吗，奥特罗？」
「*是，先生。*」
「怎么样，」维托里说，「要送酒上来吗？」

===Original===
“Sure,” said Joe, “send me up a snort. I guess Rico’ll take milk.” Rico didn’t drink.
“All right about Rico,” said Vettori in a good humour, “he’s a smart boy.”
Tony went out followed by Vettori.
“I think I go see my woman,” said Otero.
Joe laughed. Rico said:
“Goodbye, Otero. Give Seal Skin a rub for me.”
===Chinese===
「当然，」乔说，「给我送杯烈酒上来。我猜里科要牛奶。」里科不喝酒。
「里科就随他，」维托里心情不错地说，「他是个机灵的小子。」
托尼出去，维托里跟着。
「我看我也去看我的女人。」奥特罗说。
乔笑了。里科说：
「回见，奥特罗。替我揉一揉海豹皮（Seal Skin）。」

===Original===
When Otero had gone, Joe said:
“Has old Seal Skin got The Greek hooked yet?”
“Well,” said Rico, “he spends a lot of jack on her. She ain’t much to look at and she’s pretty old, but what’s the difference?”
Joe never could figure Rico out. Women didn’t seem to interest him.
Rico went over to the window and stood looking out at the electric sign on a level with his eyes.
===Chinese===
奥特罗走后，乔说：
「老海豹皮把希腊人钓上手了吗？」
「唉，」里科说，「他在她身上砸了不少钱。她模样不怎么样，年纪也不小了，可那有什么要紧？」
乔始终摸不透里科。女人似乎引不起他的兴趣。
里科踱到窗前，站着眺望那跟他眼睛齐平的霓虹招牌。

===Original===
> Club 

 P 

 a 

 l 

 e 

 r 

 m 

 o
> Dancing
===Chinese===
> 俱乐部 

 帕

 勒

 莫
> 跳舞

===Original===
Rico and Joe felt queer alone together. They were silent. Joe took out his gold cigarette case and lit a cigarette. Snow began to fall past the window.
“Look,” said Rico, “it’s snowing.”
“Yeah,” said Joe, looking up mechanically, “snowing hard.”
===Chinese===
单独待在一起，里科和乔都觉得别扭。两人都不说话。乔掏出金烟盒，点上一支烟。雪开始飘落窗前。
「瞧，」里科说，「下雪了。」
「嗯，」乔机械地抬头看了一眼，「下大了。」

### II / 二

===Original===
Vettori was sitting in his little office on the main floor. On the other side of the wall the jazz band was playing, but he paid no attention. The noise of the jazz band was the same to him as the ticking of a clock to an ordinary person. He felt very pleasant and comfortable over his bottle of wine and his plate of spaghetti. Things were right!
He congratulated himself on his subordinates. Each man a specialist. Yes, yes! That was the way to do. None of this hit or miss stuff for Sam Vettori. Rico the best gunman in Little Italy; a swelled head, all right, but he can be handled, and there you are! Otero so crazy about Rico he “don’t know nothing.” Follow Rico any place; do anything Rico tells him. And handy with a rod. Well, well. Not bad for a Mexican. As a rule foreigners were not right with Sam Vettori, but in general he had an open mind, and Otero was the goods. And look at Joe Massara, there was a man for you! A swell Italian who could pass anywhere. One winter in Florida, so they say, Joe passed himself off as a count and hooked a rich widow for plenty. Yes, yes! That was Joe for you. As an inside man you couldn’t beat him. And Tony! He could drive a car sixty miles an hour straight up the Tribune Tower. Only one thing, sometimes Tony was undependable. Used to be a choir boy at St. Dominick’s and that stuff. But he had outgrown that, maybe; anyway he was dead scared of Rico and that would shut his mouth.
Vettori leaned back, wiped his mouth with the back of his hand and unbuttoned his vest. Spaghetti and wine, what is better!
===Chinese===
维托里坐在主楼面自己的小办公室里。墙那边爵士乐队正演奏着，他却毫不在意。爵士乐队的吵闹于他，就像时钟的滴答于常人。他守着一瓶葡萄酒、一盘意大利面，觉得又惬意又舒坦。事情对路了！
他很为自己的手下得意。个个都是行家。是啊，是啊！就该这么干。萨姆·维托里可不搞那些瞎碰运气的勾当。里科是小意大利（Little Italy）最好的枪手；脑子是大了点，没错，可他驾驭得住，这不就成了！奥特罗迷里科迷得「什么都不知道」。里科去哪儿他跟到哪儿；里科叫他干啥他干啥。而且使枪利索。嗯，嗯。对一个墨西哥人来说不算坏了。照理说外国佬跟萨姆·维托里不对付，不过大体上他心胸是开阔的，奥特罗是块好料。再瞧瞧乔·马萨拉，那才叫人物！一个体面的意大利人，哪儿都吃得开。据说有一年冬天在佛罗里达（Florida），乔冒充伯爵，钓上一个有钱的寡妇，大捞了一笔。是啊，是啊！乔就是这么号人。当内应没人比得上他。还有托尼！他能开着车以每小时六十英里直冲论坛报大楼（Tribune Tower）。只有一样，托尼有时候靠不住。从前在圣多米尼克教堂（St. Dominick’s）当过唱诗班男童，诸如此类。不过那阶段兴许已经过去了；反正他怕里科怕得要死，这就封得住他的嘴。
维托里往后一靠，用手背抹了抹嘴，解开马甲的扣子。意大利面配葡萄酒，还有更美的吗！

===Original===
The band stopped playing. Bat Carillo, the bouncer, put his head in the door.
“Couple of hard guys looking for trouble, boss,” he said.
Vettori looked up.
“Yeah? Know them?”
“Never seen them before.”
Vettori heaved himself to his feet and walked with Carillo to the swinging doors which separated the backrooms and kitchen from the club proper. He pushed the door open about a foot and peered in. Carillo pointed.
===Chinese===
乐队停了演奏。看场子的巴特·卡里洛（Bat Carillo）把头探进门来。
「有俩硬茬子找事儿，老板。」他说。
维托里抬起头。
「是吗？认识吗？」
「从没见过。」
维托里撑着站起身，跟卡里洛走到那排弹簧门前——门把后屋、厨房和俱乐部正厅隔开。他把门推开约一英尺宽，朝里张望。卡里洛指了指。

===Original===
Vettori laughed and closed the door.
“Some of them dumb Irish,” he said; “let ’em alone unless they get bad and start something, then bounce ’em.”
“OK, boss,” said Carillo.
Waiters passed Vettori in the corridor, sweat dropping from their faces, steam rising from the dishes on the slanted trays. Vettori rubbed his hands.
“Business is good. Well, well! We won’t none of us die in the poor house.”
When he got back to his office he found Scabby, the informer, waiting for him. Scabby was dark and undersized with a heavy, sullen, blotched face. Passing as a police informer, he was in reality a member of the Vettori gang. He played a dangerous game as he informed on other gangs. His life wasn’t worth a cent and he was jumpy and quick with a gun.
===Chinese===
维托里笑了，关上门。
「几个蠢爱尔兰佬，」他说；「别理他们，除非他们撒野动手，那就把他们扔出去。」
「明白，老板。」卡里洛说。
侍者们从走廊里维托里身边走过，脸上淌着汗，斜托盘上的菜冒着热气。维托里搓着手。
「生意好。嗯，嗯！咱们谁也不会死在贫民院里了。」
他回到办公室，发现线人斯卡比正等着他。斯卡比皮肤黝黑，个子矮小，一张阴沉的脸上满是斑痕，显得滞重。他顶着警方线人的名头，实际上是维托里帮的成员。他干着危险的营生，向警方出卖别的帮派。他的命不值一文，人又神经质，拔枪飞快。

===Original===
“Well, well, Giovanni,” said Vettori, “what’s the news?”
“Everything’s jake,” said Scabby, taking off his hat and revealing a shining bald head.
Vettori called a waiter.
“Some spaghetti for this man here,” he said, “and a bottle of wine.”
“That’s the ticket,” said Scabby without smiling; he never smiled; his face was melancholy and lined, and sagged like a hound’s. “The boys on?”
“All set,” said Vettori. “It looks easy.”
===Chinese===
「嗯，嗯，乔瓦尼（Giovanni），」维托里说，「有什么消息？」
「一切妥当。」斯卡比说，摘下帽子，露出一颗油亮的秃头。
维托里唤来一名侍者。
「给这位来份意大利面，」他说，「再来一瓶葡萄酒。」
「这就对了。」斯卡比说，脸上没有笑意；他从来不笑；他的脸忧郁多皱，像猎犬的脸一样松垂着。「弟兄们干不干？」
「都齐了。」维托里说。「看着容易。」

===Original===
Scabby nodded.
“It ought to be. But no gunplay, get that, Sam. The Big Boy’d raise hell if he knew what was up.”
Vettori’s face hardened.
“I heard that once, Scabby. That’s enough. This is too good to pass up.”
“All right,” said Scabby, “I’ve had my say. But things ain’t what they used to be, Sam. It’s getting dangerous. They’ve even got the Big Boy scared. It’s the damn newspapers. They play that crime stuff off the boards. Big headlines, see? That’s the trouble.”
They sat silent. Vettori, absorbed, puffed on his stogie. Finally he said:
===Chinese===
斯卡比点点头。
「本来就该容易。但别开枪，记住，萨姆。大人物要是知道内情，会闹翻天的。」
维托里的脸沉了下来。
「这话我听过一回，斯卡比。够了。这票好得不能错过。」
「行吧，」斯卡比说，「我的话说完了。可世道不比从前了，萨姆。越来越危险。连大人物都给吓着了。都怪那些该死的报纸。他们把犯罪那套炒得翻了天。大标题，瞧见没？麻烦就在这儿。」
两人坐着没说话。维托里出神地抽着雪茄。末了他说：

===Original===
“Listen, Scabby, you ain’t heard nothing, see? I got to keep these boys on their toes. Especially Joe. Don’t spill nothing about the Big Boy.”
Scabby shook his head vigorously. Vettori took out his billfold and handed Scabby a fifty.
“That’s part of your split, Scabby. Keep your eyes open, that’s all.”
Scabby pocketed the money. The waiter came in bringing the spaghetti and the wine. Carillo put his head in the door.
“Reilley, the dick’s, up front.”
===Chinese===
「听着，斯卡比，你什么也没听见，明白？我得让这帮小子绷紧神经。尤其是乔。大人物的事一个字也别漏。」
斯卡比使劲摇头。维托里掏出钱夹，递给斯卡比一张五十的。
「这是你分成的一部分，斯卡比。把眼睛放亮，就这些。」
斯卡比把钱揣进口袋。侍者端着意大利面和葡萄酒进来。卡里洛把头探进门来。
「雷利（Reilley），那警探，在前头。」

===Original===
Vettori nodded.
“He’s OK. If he sticks around, send him back in about half an hour.”
“Sure,” said Scabby, “I’ll be out of here in less than that.”
===Chinese===
维托里点点头。
「他没问题。他要是赖着不走，过半个钟头让他到后头来。」
「行，」斯卡比说，「我用不了那么久就走。」

### III / 三

===Original===
Otero lay looking out the window at the electric sign across the Street.
===Chinese===
奥特罗躺着，望向窗外街对面的霓虹招牌。

===Original===
> Club 

 P 

 a 

 l 

 e 

 r 

 m 

 o
> Dancing
===Chinese===
> 俱乐部 

 帕

 勒

 莫
> 跳舞

===Original===
Snow was falling past the windows. Otero lay smoking a big twenty-five cent cigar and singing softly to himself. He always sang when he was with Seal Skin.
“Some snow,” said Otero.
“Yeah, some snow,” said Seal Skin, who was sitting with her feet on the window sill, smoking one of Otero’s cigars.
“Heavy like cotton,” said Otero.
“Yeah,” said Seal Skin.
“Where I used to be it never snowed.”
===Chinese===
雪从窗外飘落。奥特罗躺着，抽一支两毛五的粗雪茄，轻声给自己哼着歌。跟海豹皮在一起时他总是唱歌。
「好大的雪。」奥特罗说。
「是啊，好大的雪。」海豹皮说，她两脚搁在窗台上，抽着奥特罗的一支雪茄。
「沉得像棉花。」奥特罗说。
「是啊。」海豹皮说。
「我从前待的地方从来不下雪。」

===Original===
“Didn’t it?”
“No, it never snowed.”
Seal Skin blew out a cloud of smoke.
“How come you ever left Mexico, Ramón?”
“Well, I don’t know.” Otero scratched his head. “I just left.”
“Was they after you?”
===Chinese===
「不下吗？」
「不，从来不下。」
海豹皮喷出一团烟。
「你到底怎么离开墨西哥（Mexico）的，拉蒙（Ramón）？」
「唉，我也说不上。」奥特罗挠了挠头。「就是走了。」
「有人追你？」

===Original===
“No, I just left.”
Otero got up and put his arms around Seal Skin.
“Some girl,” he said.
Seal Skin gave him a push.
“Wait’ll I finish this.”
“Sure, sure,” said Otero, smiling and patting her on the shoulder.
===Chinese===
「没有，就是走了。」
奥特罗起身，搂住海豹皮。
「好姑娘。」他说。
海豹皮推开他。
「等我把这支抽完。」
「行，行。」奥特罗说，笑着拍拍她的肩膀。

===Original===
“Look,” said Seal Skin, “you’re a good guy, Ramón. But dumb. How come you hang after Rico?”
“Rico is a great man.”
Seal Skin laughed out loud.
“Yeah? Great, but careless. He’ll never die of old age.”
Otero didn’t understand.
“What you say?”
===Chinese===
「听着，」海豹皮说，「你是个好人，拉蒙。可就是蠢。你怎么老是跟着里科转？」
「里科是了不起的人物。」
海豹皮放声大笑。
「是吗？了不起，可不小心。他决活不到老死。」
奥特罗不明白。
「你说什么？」

===Original===
“They’ll fill him full of lead. He’s too cocky.”
Otero shook his head.
“No, they’ll never get Rico.”
“They get ’em all.”
“No,” said Otero, “they’ll never get Rico. Once I say to him. ‘Look, you must be careful.’ But he say, ‘Not me, they’ll never get Rico.’ ”
Seal Skin opened the window and flung her cigar down into the street. A gust of cold air rushed into the overheated room.
===Chinese===
「他们会把他打成筛子。他太自大了。」
奥特罗摇头。
「不，他们永远抓不到里科。」
「他们谁都能收拾。」
「不，」奥特罗说，「他们永远抓不到里科。有一回我跟他说：『听着，你得小心。』可他说：『我不用，他们永远抓不到里科。』」
海豹皮推开窗，把雪茄扔到街上。一股冷风灌进这间过热的屋子。

===Original===
“Listen,” she said, “that’s bunk. Rico’s no different from anybody else. You stick with Rico long enough and you’ll have a swell funeral. Why don’t you get into the beer racket? That’s safe.”
“I go with Rico,” said Otero. “What do I care? I have no people. Once I had a brother but they shot him.”
“The cops?”
“No, the *rurales*. He was with Villa.”
“Who the hell’s Villa?”
“Villa was a great man, like Rico.”
===Chinese===
「听着，」她说，「那是鬼话。里科跟别人没什么两样。你跟里科跟得够久，就会有一场风光的葬礼。你怎么不干私酒这行？那个安全。」
「我跟里科走。」奥特罗说。「我在乎什么？我孤身一人。从前有个哥哥，可他们把他打死了。」
「警察？」
「不，是乡警（*rurales*）。他跟着维拉（Villa）干。」
「维拉他妈的是谁？」
「维拉是了不起的人物，跟里科一样。」

===Original===
Seal Skin got up and took a drink from a bottle on the bureau. Then she said:
“Let’s hit the hay, Ramón.”
“Sure, sure,” said Otero.
===Chinese===
海豹皮起身，从柜子上的酒瓶里倒了一口喝。然后她说：
「睡吧，拉蒙。」
「行，行。」奥特罗说。

### IV / 四

===Original===
It was nearly two o’clock when Tony left his woman. A lake wind was blowing hard and the snow fell heavily past the street lights. Tony muffled himself in his overcoat and pulled his cap low. He felt tired and disgusted.
At the corner near his home, he turned into Sicily Pete’s restaurant. Three Italians were playing cards at a table in the back. Up front a mechanical piano ground out “The Rosary.”
“Hello, Tony, how’s the boy?” said Pete.
“Not so good,” said Tony.
“You ain’t looking any too good, Tony,” said Pete.
Tony ran his hands over his face and stared at his image in the mirror behind the counter. Pale; circles under his eyes.
===Chinese===
托尼离开他的女人时快两点了。湖风刮得紧，大雪在路灯前沉沉地落。托尼用大衣裹紧自己，把帽檐压得很低。他又觉得累，又觉得腻烦。
在离家不远的街角，他拐进了西西里人皮特（Sicily Pete）的饭馆。后头一张桌边有三个意大利人在打牌。店堂前面，一架自动钢琴磨着《玫瑰经》（The Rosary）。
「你好啊，托尼，小子怎么样？」皮特说。
「不怎么样。」托尼说。
「你气色也不怎么好，托尼。」皮特说。
托尼双手抹了把脸，盯着柜台后镜子里的自己。苍白；眼圈发黑。

===Original===
“Well, I guess I’ll live,” said Tony.
Pete smacked the counter with both hands.
“Love of God! Sure you’ll live. You be OK tomorrow morning. I know, Tony, my boy. Don’t forget I was a young fellow once. I know. I know.”
“Sure you know,” said Tony, sarcastic.
“Sure I do. You think I don’t know about that little red head. Hot stuff, Tony, my boy. Only don’t be a fool. Save some for tomorrow night.”
Pete laughed shaking all over and smacked the counter with his hands.
===Chinese===
「唉，大概还死不了。」托尼说。
皮特双手啪地拍在柜台上。
「看在老天爷份上！你当然死不了。明儿早上你就没事了。我知道的，托尼，我的小子。别忘了我也年轻过。我知道。我知道。」
「你当然知道。」托尼说，话里带刺。
「我当然知道。你以为我不晓得那个小红毛的事。够辣的，托尼，我的小子。只是别犯傻。给明晚留点儿劲儿。」
皮特笑得浑身直颤，双手拍着柜台。

===Original===
“What the hell’s wrong with you, Pete?” cried one of the card players.
“Never you mind. All right, Tony, what’ll you have?”
Tony couldn’t decide. Pete went to wait on one of the card players. The mechanical piano finished “The Rosary” on a discord. Tony went over and put a nickel in the slot.
“I got a combination to go and two Javas,” said Pete. The mechanical piano began to play “O Sole Mio.”
“I’ll take a combination,” said Tony, “and a cup of Java.”
“OK,” said Pete. “I got two combinations, one to go and three on the Java.”
===Chinese===
「你他妈的犯什么毛病，皮特？」一个打牌的嚷道。
「不用你管。好了，托尼，来点什么？」
托尼拿不定主意。皮特去招呼一个打牌的了。自动钢琴在一声走调的和弦里结束了《玫瑰经》。托尼走过去，往投币口塞了一枚五分镍币。
「我记下一份外带的套餐和两杯咖啡。」皮特说。自动钢琴开始奏《我的太阳》（O Sole Mio）。
「我来一份套餐，」托尼说，「再来一杯咖啡。」
「好嘞。」皮特说。「两份套餐，一份外带，三杯咖啡。」

===Original===
“How’s business, Pete?” asked Tony.
“Oh, what you call so-so. Not good, not bad. I never get rich here.”
“Why don’t you put in a line of bottled goods?” said Tony, smiling.
Pete raised both hands over his head and brought them down hard on the counter.
“None of that for Sicily Pete. Oh no. Pete’s too smart for that. If the bulls don’t get you, why, some of them gangsters do. I know. One say, you buy from me; the other say, no, you buy from me. All the same. No matter who you buy from, bango!”
Pete brought Tony his combination and his coffee, and stood at the counter with him while he ate it.
===Chinese===
「生意怎么样，皮特？」托尼问。
「唉，就那样吧。不好，不坏。我在这儿永远发不了财。」
「你怎么不进一批瓶装货卖卖？」托尼笑着说。
皮特双手举过头顶，又重重拍到柜台上。
「西西里人皮特可不干那个。噢，不干。皮特没那么傻。条子不收拾你，那些匪帮也要收拾你。我知道的。这个说，你进我的货；那个说，不行，你进我的货。一个样。不管你进谁的货，砰！」
皮特把套餐和咖啡端给托尼，站在柜台边陪着他吃。

===Original===
“Tony,” said Pete, putting his head on one side, “you know you look like your old man. Other day when you was in here I say to the missus, ‘look, ain’t he just like his old man?’ Well, well. That is good. A boy should look like his old man. That is a good sign.”
“Knew the old man pretty well, didn’t you, Pete?” said Tony, finishing his coffee.
“Yes, pretty well. When he was a young fellow he was like you. Full of pep and always after the girls. But I don’t know, your mama she got hold of him, then he wasn’t like he used to be. He wasn’t like the same fellow. Pretty soon he died.”
Tony laughed.
“Hard on the old lady, ain’t you?”
“Love of God, no,” said Pete, an expression of acute misery on his face, “you don’t get me, Tony. I mean he got to be a good fellow, like me. Work, work, that’s all he knew. Well, work is a good thing. It keeps you out of trouble, but I don’t know.”
===Chinese===
「托尼，」皮特说，头歪向一边，「你知道你长得像你老子。前几天你在这儿的时候我跟老板娘说：『瞧，他是不是跟他老子一个模子？』嗯，嗯。这很好。小子就该像老子。这是好兆头。」
「跟我老子挺熟的吧，皮特？」托尼说，喝完咖啡。
「熟，挺熟。他年轻那会儿跟你一样。浑身是劲，老是追姑娘。可是怎么说呢，你妈把他攥住了，他就不像从前那个他了。像换了个人似的。没过多久就死了。」
托尼笑了。
「你这话对老太太可够狠的，嗯？」
「看在老天爷份上，不是，」皮特说，一脸的痛苦，「你误会我了，托尼。我是说他后来成了个好人，像我一样。干活，干活，他就知道干活。唉，干活是好事。能让人不惹麻烦，不过，我也说不好。」

===Original===
Pete wiped the sweat from his forehead and meditated. Tony flipped him a fifty-cent piece. The mechanical piano stopped on a prolonged, slurred discord.
“Well, I guess I’ll hit for home,” said Tony, “so long, Pete.”
“Good night, Tony,” said Pete, with one of his blandest smiles, “come in again.”
The wind struck Tony in the face as he left the restaurant. The streets were white and silent. Tony walked home slowly, tired and disgusted.
As he entered the flat he saw a dim light in the front room. He tried to sneak into his bedroom, but his mother heard him. She rose from her chair, a monstrous silhouette against the dim front room light.
“A fine time for you to come in, Antonio,” she said. “Have you been out again with them good-for-nothing loafers?”
===Chinese===
皮特擦掉额头上的汗，出了会儿神。托尼弹给他一枚五毛硬币。自动钢琴在一声拖长的、含混的走调和弦中停了下来。
「唉，我该回家了，」托尼说，「回见，皮特。」
「晚安，托尼。」皮特说，带着他最温吞和气的笑，「再来啊。」
托尼一出饭馆，风就扑到脸上。街道一片白，一片静。托尼慢慢走回家去，又累又腻烦。
他一进公寓就看见前屋亮着一盏昏灯。他想溜进自己的卧室，可他母亲听见了他。她从椅子上站起来，映着前屋的昏光，像一个庞大的黑影。
「你倒会挑好时辰回家，安东尼奥（Antonio）。」她说。「又跟那些不中用的游手好闲鬼混去了？」

===Original===
“Yes,” said Tony, in a bad temper.
“So﻿ ﻿… !” said his mother, “you don’t even lie any more. Well, well! You are doing fine. Pretty soon you won’t come home at all, you bum.”
“You said a mouthful,” said Tony.
“Sure, you won’t listen to your mother. Some day you’ll remember what I told you. You loaf with crooks and bums long enough, you’ll see what will happen.”
“All right,” said Tony, going into his room and banging the door behind him.
His mother stood in the middle of the room for a minute, then she put out the light and sat in the dark, crying.
===Chinese===
「是。」托尼说，火气很冲。
「这么说……！」他母亲说，「你连撒谎都懒得撒了。好啊，好啊！你出息了。用不了多久你就干脆不回家了，你这个二流子。」
「你说得一点没错。」托尼说。
「可不是，你就是不听你妈的话。早晚有一天你会想起我说的话。你跟骗子二流子混得够久了，有你瞧的。」
「行吧。」托尼说着进了自己房间，砰地甩上门。
他母亲在屋子中间站了片刻，然后熄了灯，坐在黑暗里哭泣。

### V / 五

===Original===
The little blonde check-girl helped Joe take off his big ulster, her hand lingering on his arm. He handed her a quarter.
“Don’t go on a bat with that two-bits,” he said.
“No, sir,” said the check-girl.
She watched him walk across the long dance-floor, pick his way among the crowded tables, bowing from time to time to one he had jostled, and disappear through the employees’ door at the back. Then she put checks on his coat and hat and hung them up.
“God, what a hot-looking man,” she said; “I don’t see how that little hunky got him.”
Olga Stassoff was just putting the finishing touches to her makeup. Joe came in softly and stood watching her. She began to sing. “If you’re singing for me,” said Joe, “you can stop any time.” Olga turned around.
===Chinese===
金发小衣帽女郎帮乔脱下他那件宽大的粗呢大衣，手在他胳膊上流连。他递给她两毛五。
「别拿这两毛五去灌黄汤。」他说。
「不会的，先生。」衣帽女郎说。
她望着他走过长长的舞池，在拥挤的餐桌间择路穿行，不时向被自己挤碰的人点头致意，然后从后头的员工门消失。随后她给他的大衣和帽子别上号牌，挂了起来。
「天哪，这男人多带劲，」她说；「真不明白那小东欧佬是怎么把他弄到手的。」
奥尔加·斯塔索夫正在给妆容做最后的修饰。乔轻轻走进来，站在一旁看她。她唱起歌来。「你要是唱给我听的，」乔说，「随时可以停下。」奥尔加转过身。

===Original===
“Well, what are you doing here? Broke?”
“Shut up,” said Joe.
Then he turned and walked out of the room. Olga jumped to her feet and ran after him. She caught him near the employees’ door. He pushed her away.
“Ain’t that a fine way to say hello to a guy!” he said. “Why, you must think you got me roped and hog-tied.”
“I was just kidding, Joe,” said Olga, “honest I didn’t mean it. I was just kidding.”
“Well, get this,” said Joe, “I’m goddamn sick of that line. What do you take me for? That goes big with some of your swell boyfriends who’ve got ugly wives and ain’t any too particular, but me! I don’t take that kind of talk from nobody.”
===Chinese===
「怎么，你来干什么？没钱了？」
「闭嘴。」乔说。
然后他转身走出房间。奥尔加跳起来追他。她在员工门附近追上他。他推开她。
「这就算跟人打招呼的好法子！」他说。「得，你八成以为把我捆得死死的了。」
「我只是开玩笑，乔，」奥尔加说，「真的没别的意思。只是开玩笑。」
「行，听清楚，」乔说，「这套话我他妈听腻了。你把我当什么人？你那些体面男朋友吃这套，他们家里老婆丑，自己也马虎得很，可我不行！这种话谁也别想对我说。」

===Original===
Olga put her arms around him, but he pushed her away.
“Listen, Joe,” she said, “I got good news for you, so get out of your fighting clothes and come to earth. Can’t you take a little kidding?”
Joe gave the manager a most ingratiating smile.
“What’s the big talk, Mr. DeVoss? Am I missing something?”
“You sure are,” said DeVoss. “The Stranskys broke their contract and I’m putting you on in their place.”
Joe leapt into the air and executed a twinkle. Olga burst out laughing.
===Chinese===
奥尔加搂住他，他又把她推开。
「听着，乔，」她说，「我有好消息给你，收起你这副打架的架势，落回地面上来。开句玩笑都不行吗？」
乔朝经理堆出一个极其讨好的笑。
「谈什么大买卖呢，德沃斯先生（Mr. DeVoss）？我是不是错过了什么？」
「可不，错过了。」德沃斯（DeVoss）说。「斯特兰斯基夫妇（The Stranskys）毁了约，我打算让你们顶他们的缺。」
乔蹦到半空，旋了一个花步。奥尔加放声大笑。

===Original===
“Well,” said Joe, “how much?”
“One hundred to start, Joe, then we’ll see.”
“Well,” said Joe, “I can’t buy no limousines with that, but I’ll take it.”
Joe and DeVoss shook hands.
“Now,” said the manager, “there’s a girl out here who’s just dying to dance with you, Joe.”
Joe shook his head.
===Chinese===
「那么，」乔说，「多少钱？」
「起先一百，乔，以后再看。」
「行，」乔说，「这钱买不起豪华轿车，但我接了。」
乔和德沃斯握了握手。
「是这样，」经理说，「外头有位小姐盼着跟你跳舞想得要命，乔。」
乔摇头。

===Original===
“No, I don’t like that stuff. They always think they got to hand you something. What the hell! I don’t want no dame handing me nothing.”
Olga put her hand over her mouth.
“Don’t worry about that, Joe,” said DeVoss, “she already asked me about that and I told her you’d be insulted, so she gave me a ten.” DeVoss took a crumpled bill out of his pocket and handed it to Joe. “There, now get this. She’s an up and up girl and she means a lot of business to this place. Her old man’s got a couple of million bucks and she’s the real thing. All right, Joe?”
“Sure, sure,” said Joe, “always willing to oblige.”
DeVoss went through the swinging doors and stood waiting for Joe on the other side. Olga took Joe by the arm.
“Listen,” she said, “none of your funny business now. Just do your stuff and leave it at that. I’m on to these society women. I know what they want.”
===Chinese===
「不，我不喜欢那一套。她们总以为得塞给你点什么。见鬼！我不要任何娘们儿塞我什么东西。」
奥尔加捂住嘴。
「这你放心，乔，」德沃斯说，「她早问过我这个，我说你会觉得受了侮辱，所以她给了我一张十块的。」德沃斯从口袋里掏出一张皱巴巴的票子递给乔。「喏，听清楚。她是个正派姑娘，对这地方的生意举足轻重。她老子有几百万块，她是货真价实的大家小姐。行吧，乔？」
「行，行，」乔说，「随时乐意效劳。」
德沃斯穿过弹簧门，站在另一边等乔。奥尔加一把拉住乔的胳膊。
「听着，」她说，「别来你那些鬼花样。就跳你的舞，别的免谈。这种上流社会的女人我看得透。我知道她们要什么。」

===Original===
Joe leapt into the air and executed another twinkle.
“Alley up!” he cried, “don’t you trust me, baby?”
Olga put her hands on her hips and began to laugh. How could you be sore at a guy like that?
===Chinese===
乔又蹦到半空，又旋了一个花步。
「嗨——哟！」他喊道，「你还不信我吗，宝贝？」
奥尔加双手叉腰，笑了起来。这样的男人，你怎么气得起来？

### VI / 六

===Original===
Rico was standing in front of his mirror, combing his hair with a little ivory pocket comb. Rico was vain of his hair. It was black and lustrous, combed straight back from his low forehead and arranged in three symmetrical waves.
Rico was a simple man. He loved but three things: himself, his hair and his gun. He took excellent care of all three.
===Chinese===
里科站在自己的镜子前，用一把小象牙随身梳梳着头发。里科对自己的头发很得意。头发乌黑发亮，从低平的前额笔直往后梳，梳成三道对称的波纹。
里科是个简单的人。他只爱三样东西：他自己、他的头发和他的枪。这三样他都精心照料。

## Part II / 第二部

### I / 一

===Original===
“Hear me,” said Rico, his face twitching, “he’s turned yellow. He’s turned yellow. What the hell you expect from a choir boy!”
Otero said nothing but sat with his chair tipped back against the wall smoking a cigarette, his eyes closed. Sam Vettori stood in the middle of the room and stared at his watch.
“Keep your shirt on, Rico,” said Vettori, “you’re on edge.”
“Sure, Rico,” said Otero.
Carillo came in without knocking. Vettori put away his watch.
“Well!”
===Chinese===
「听见没，」里科说，脸抽动着，「他怂了。他怂了。一个唱诗班出身的小子，你还能指望个屁！」
奥特罗没说话，只是椅子往后翘着靠墙坐着，抽烟，闭着眼。萨姆·维托里站在屋子当中，盯着表看。
「沉住气，里科，」维托里说，「你太紧张了。」
「就是，里科。」奥特罗说。
卡里洛没敲门就进来了。维托里收起表。
「怎么样！」

===Original===
“OK, boss,” said Carillo, “Tony’s in the alley.”
Vettori took out his watch again.
“Rico, it’s eleven-thirty-five. What do you say?”
“Let’s get going.”
Otero got slowly to his feet, stamped out his cigarette, and taking the riot gun from the table in front of him, slipped it under his overcoat. Rico examined his big automatic.
Carillo went out, softly closing the door. Otero walked over and patted Rico on the shoulder.
===Chinese===
「好了，老板，」卡里洛说，「托尼在巷子里。」
维托里又掏出表来。
「里科，十一点三十五了。你怎么说？」
「走吧。」
奥特罗慢慢站起来，踩灭烟头，从面前的桌上拿起防暴霰弹枪，掖进大衣底下。里科检视着他的大号自动手枪。
卡里洛出去，轻轻带上门。奥特罗走过去拍拍里科的肩膀。

===Original===
“OK, now, eh, Rico?”
Rico smiled. Vettori’s face was covered with sweat and he pulled out a big white silk handkerchief to mop it.
“Rico,” he said, “from now on you boss the job. Only, get this: for the love of God, no gunwork. That’s all. I ain’t ripe for the rope.”
Rico said nothing. Otero shrugged.
Vettori, still mopping his face, opened a window and a gust of cold air rushed in.
Rico took out his little ivory pocket comb and mechanically combed his hair. Then he put on his hat and tilted it over his eyes.
===Chinese===
「成了，嗯，里科？」
里科笑了。维托里满脸是汗，掏出一块白绸大手帕来擦。
「里科，」他说，「从现在起这活儿你当家。只是记住：看在老天爷份上，别开枪。就这一条。我还不打算上绞架。」
里科没说话。奥特罗耸耸肩。
维托里还擦着脸，推开一扇窗，一股冷风灌了进来。
里科掏出那把小象牙随身梳，机械地梳了梳头发。然后戴上帽子，帽檐压到眼睛上。

===Original===
“Well,” he said to Otero, “let’s go.”
Otero followed Rico out. Vettori called:
“Make it clean, Rico. Make it clean.”
They went down the back stairs. Carillo was waiting at the foot of the stairs and held the alley door open for them. The alleyway was dark and Otero stumbled.
“*Caramba!*”
“Watch that gun,” said Rico.
===Chinese===
「行了，」他对奥特罗说，「走。」
奥特罗跟着里科出去。维托里喊道：
「干利索点，里科。干利索点。」
他们下了后楼梯。卡里洛在楼梯脚下等着，给他们拉开通巷子的门。巷子里黑，奥特罗绊了一下。
「*该死！*」
「看着点枪。」里科说。

===Original===
Tony was sitting at the wheel of a big, open Cadillac. He tossed his cigarette away and said:
“Well, here we are.”
Rico said nothing, but got into the front seat with Tony. Otero got into the back seat. Carillo stood looking at them for a moment, then closed the door. Tony stepped on the starter.
“All right,” said Rico, “let’s go, but take it easy. We got lots of time.”
They took it easy. Tony drove along as leisurely as though they were going to a New Year’s party. Rico leaned back and smoked, watching all the passing cars. Otero, who had removed the riot gun and had it on the seat beside him, was sitting bolt upright, his hands on his knees. He could never get used to riding in an automobile. Rico turned and saw the gun.
“Put that rod on the floor,” he said.
===Chinese===
托尼坐在一辆敞篷大凯迪拉克（Cadillac）的方向盘后。他把烟一扔，说：
「好，都齐了。」
里科没说话，上了前座，跟托尼坐一起。奥特罗坐进后座。卡里洛站着看了他们片刻，然后关上门。托尼踩下启动踏板。
「行了，」里科说，「走，不过别急。时间有的是。」
他们真就不急。托尼一路开得悠闲，仿佛是去赴新年晚会。里科往后靠着抽烟，看着过往的车辆。奥特罗把霰弹枪挪出来搁在旁边座位上，人笔直坐着，双手放在膝盖上。他永远习惯不了坐汽车。里科回头看见了枪。
「把枪放地板上。」他说。

===Original===
Otero obeyed.
It had got colder. The snow was no longer falling and a chilly wind was blowing up in gusts from the lake. The streets were nearly deserted. Over west a whistle began to blow, discordant and shrill.
“Well,” said Tony, nodding in the direction of the whistle, “it won’t be long now.”
But Rico leaned over and hissed in his ear.
“Police car!”
A big Packard with a hooded machine-gun in the back seat passed them. There were two plainclothes men in the front and two in the back.
===Chinese===
奥特罗照办了。
天更冷了。雪停了，一股股阴冷的风从湖上刮来。街上几乎没了人影。西边远处响起一声哨子，尖利刺耳。
「嘿，」托尼说着，朝哨声的方向点了点头，「快了。」
里科却俯过身，在他耳边低声道：
「警车！」
一辆大帕卡德（Packard）从他们旁边开过去，后座上架着一挺罩着枪衣的机关枪。前排坐着两个便衣，后排也是两个。

===Original===
“What’ll I do?” asked Tony.
One of the men leaned out and stared back at them.
“Jesus,” said Tony, “he’s looking at us.”
“Keep your shirt on,” said Rico, putting his hand on Tony’s arm.
Otero took a cigarette from his pack and rolled it between his palms.
The police car slowed up. Rico’s fingers closed on Tony’s arm.
===Chinese===
「我怎么办？」托尼问。
一个便衣探出身来，回头盯着他们。
「老天，」托尼说，「他在看咱们。」
「沉住气。」里科说，把手搭在托尼胳膊上。
奥特罗从烟盒里抽出一支烟，在两只手掌间搓着。
警车放慢了。里科的手指抠紧了托尼的胳膊。

===Original===
“Here’s an alley,” said Rico, “duck!”
Tony took the turn on two wheels, just missing a parked car. Otero was thrown from one end of his seat to the other, losing his cigarette. The Cadillac’s exhaust roared in the narrow alleyway. There was nothing but darkness ahead of them.
“It’s a blind,” said Tony.
“No,” said Rico, “I know this place like a book. Turn to your right at the end.”
Rico leaned out and stared back. Then he laughed.
“Ain’t that like the damn dummies! Nothing in sight.”
===Chinese===
「这儿有条巷子，」里科说，「拐！」
托尼两轮着地硬拐过弯，差点撞上一辆停着的车。奥特罗从座位这头甩到那头，烟也丢了。凯迪拉克的排气在窄巷里轰鸣。前方一片漆黑。
「这是条死巷。」托尼说。
「不，」里科说，「这地方我熟得跟一本书似的。到头往右拐。」
里科探出头去回望。然后他笑了。
「这帮蠢货就是这个德行！影子都没有。」

===Original===
They came back to Michigan Boulevard by a wide detour. Here the wind blew fiercely, raising little whirlwinds of snow. Now there were whistles blowing in all parts of town. Rico looked at his wrist watch.
“Five of twelve. All right, Tony. Step on it.”
“What time, Rico?” asked Otero.
Rico told him.
“Fine, fine,” said Otero, “eh, Rico?”
Half a block down the street they saw the huge electric sign of the Casa Alvarado. The street was deserted except for the parked cars. They drove along slowly now.
===Chinese===
他们绕了个大弯回到密歇根大道（Michigan Boulevard）。这里风刮得猛，卷起一个个小小的雪漩。这时全城各处都响起了哨子。里科看了看腕表。
「十二点差五分。行了，托尼。踩油门。」
「几点了，里科？」奥特罗问。
里科告诉了他。
「好，好。」奥特罗说，「嗯，里科？」
沿街再走半条街，他们看见了卡萨·阿尔瓦拉多的巨大霓虹招牌。街上空无一人，只有停着的汽车。这会儿他们开得很慢。

===Original===
Rico leaned out.
“That’s a break,” he said, pointing to a parking place where they couldn’t be hemmed in. “Listen, Tony, this ain’t going to be no cinch, so you better give us a lift.”
Tony pretended to be preoccupied with parking.
“Get me?”
Tony was pale and his lips were twitching.
“That ain’t my stand, Rico,” he said.
===Chinese===
里科探出身去。
「运气不错。」他说，指着一个不会被堵死的停车位。「听着，托尼，这活儿可不容易，你最好跟我们一起进去搭把手。」
托尼装作一门心思在停车。
「明白吗？」
托尼脸色发白，嘴唇抽动。
「那不是我份内的活儿，里科。」他说。

===Original===
Rico looked at him. Tony sat silent for a moment, then, pulling at the visor of his cap, said:
“But you’re the boss, Rico.”
“OK,” said Rico, smiling. “Now, Otero, get this. I go first. You follow me with the big rod. I stick up the cashier. Tony swings the sacks. Got it?” Rico took three small neatly-folded canvas sacks out of his pocket and handed them to Tony. “Otero, you watch the door. If you see anybody coming in, let ’em come in, then back ’em up against the wall. If things go right, I’ll tap the box. Got it?”
Rico looked at his watch. It was three minutes past twelve.
“Let’s go,” he said.
Otero got out lazily, hiding the riot gun under his coat. Rico got out, followed by Tony.
===Chinese===
里科看着他。托尼沉默地坐了片刻，然后拉了拉帽檐，说：
「不过你说了算，里科。」
「行。」里科说，笑了。「现在听着，奥特罗。我打头。你扛着大枪跟着我。我制服出纳。托尼拿袋子。明白？」里科从口袋里掏出三只叠得整整齐齐的小帆布袋，递给托尼。「奥特罗，你看住门。看见有人进来，就让他们进来，再把他们逼到墙根。事情顺的话，我去撬保险柜。明白？」
里科看了看表。十二点过三分。
「走。」他说。
奥特罗慢悠悠地下车，把霰弹枪藏在大衣底下。里科下了车，托尼跟着。

===Original===
“Got your rod, Tony?” asked Rico.
Tony nodded.
“All right, keep it in your pocket. Maybe you won’t need it right away. If anybody gets funny, why, pull it.”
“OK,” said Tony, “but for God’s sake, Rico, no gunwork.”
Otero said:
“You leave Rico alone. He does what is right.”
===Chinese===
「带枪了吗，托尼？」里科问。
托尼点点头。
「好，先搁在口袋里。兴许一时用不上。谁敢耍花招，就拔出来。」
「行，」托尼说，「可是看在老天爷份上，里科，别开枪。」
奥特罗说：
「你别烦里科。他做的事没有不对的。」

===Original===
Whistles were blowing all over town. They walked up the carpet which was laid across the pavement under the canvas marquee. Inside there was a blaze of lights and they could hear the music. The lobby was deserted except for two check-girls, one waiter, a cigar clerk, and the cashier, a pale woman with a green eyeshade, who was perched on a stool. Joe Massara, in a big ulster and a derby hat, was standing at the cigar counter, kidding the clerk. He saw them out of the corner of his eye and nodded twice.
They came in quickly, Rico in front with his big automatic at ready, Otero slightly behind him and to the left, carrying the sawed-off shotgun hip-high, Tony in the rear, his hand in his overcoat pocket.
Before Rico could say anything, Joe Massara faced them, put his back up against the counter and raised his hands.
“My God,” he cried, “it’s a holdup.”
One of the check girls screamed piercingly. The waiter’s knees buckled and he almost fell. The others stood petrified.
“You’re goddamn right it’s a holdup!” shouted Rico, trying to intimidate them, “and it ain’t gonna be no picnic. Get that, all of you birds. I got lead in this here rod and my finger’s itching. One crack out of any of you and they’ll pat you with a spade. All right, Tony.”
===Chinese===
满城哨子乱响。他们踏上帆布雨棚下铺过人行道的地毯。里面灯火通明，音乐声听得见。大堂里冷冷清清，只有两个衣帽女郎、一名侍者、一个雪茄柜伙计，还有出纳——一个戴绿遮光眼罩的苍白女人，坐在高凳上。乔·马萨拉穿着宽大的粗呢大衣，戴着圆顶礼帽，正站在雪茄柜台前跟伙计逗趣。他用眼角看见他们，点了两次头。
他们迅速进来，里科在前，大号自动手枪端在手里，奥特罗在他左后方半步，把锯短的霰弹枪端在胯高，托尼殿后，手插在大衣口袋里。
里科还没来得及开口，乔·马萨拉已迎着他们转过身，背靠柜台，举起了双手。
「我的天，」他喊道，「抢劫了。」
一个衣帽女郎发出刺耳的尖叫。侍者双膝一软，差点摔倒。其余的人都僵在原地。
「他妈的没错，是抢劫！」里科吼道，想镇住他们，「而且这可不是闹着玩的。都记住了，你们这帮鸟人。我这枪里灌满了铅弹，手指正痒着呢。谁敢吱一声，就让人拿铁锹拍你们。行，托尼。」

===Original===
Tony, white as chalk, took the sacks out of his pocket and walked over to the cashier’s desk. The cashier was standing behind the register, hands raised. When Tony came up she said:
“Take anything you want, only for God’s sake don’t touch me.”
“OK,” said Tony, “clean out the box but don’t get funny.”
Tony held the sacks while the cashier scooped the money into them. Tony saw pack after pack of wrapped greenbacks drop into the sacks. He began to feel a little better.
Rico left the cashier to Tony, but looked at each of the others in turn, his eyes, under his tilted hat, intimidating them as successfully as the big Luger in his hand. Otero stood behind him and a little to the left, impassive, the riot gun hip-high.
The manager opened the door of his office and with a dazed look hesitated for a moment, then, with a great sigh, put his back against the wall and raised his hands. He was a Czech with a swarthy complexion which gradually turned greenish.
===Chinese===
托尼白得像粉笔，从口袋里掏出袋子，走到出纳台前。出纳站在收银机后，双手举着。托尼走近时她说：
「要什么随便拿，只求老天爷别碰我。」
「行，」托尼说，「把柜子清空，别耍花招。」
托尼撑着袋子，出纳把钱一捧捧往里塞。托尼看着一捆捆扎好的钞票落进袋子。他开始觉得好受了点。
出纳交给托尼，里科挨个打量其余的人，压低的帽檐下那双眼睛，跟手里的大鲁格（Luger）枪一样镇得住人。奥特罗站在他身后偏左，面无表情，霰弹枪端在胯高。
经理推开办公室的门，一脸茫然地迟疑了片刻，然后长叹一声，背贴墙站住，举起了双手。他是个黑肤色的捷克人，脸色渐渐转成青绿。

===Original===
Rico glared at the manager.
“Stay put, you!” he said.
“All right, all right,” said the Czech.
Joe Massara said:
“Jesus, my arm’s paralyzed.”
“Yeah,” shouted Rico, “well, don’t let it drop.”
===Chinese===
里科瞪着经理。
「待着别动，你！」他说。
「行，行。」捷克人说。
乔·马萨拉说：
「老天，我胳膊麻了。」
「行，」里科吼道，「那就别放下来。」

===Original===
“All set,” cried Tony.
Otero was busy at the door with a man in a top hat who had just come in. The man couldn’t believe his eyes and kept muttering:
“Good Lord! Good Lord!”
Otero backed him against the wall.
In the club proper, beyond the big arched doorways, the band was playing loudly, horns were tooting, people were shouting.
“All right,” said Rico, “get out your gat, Tony. I’ll tap the box inside.”
===Chinese===
「都齐了。」托尼喊道。
奥特罗在门口正忙着对付一个刚进来的戴大礼帽的男人。那人不敢相信自己的眼睛，嘴里直念叨：
「老天爷！老天爷！」
奥特罗把他逼到墙根。
大拱门那边的俱乐部正厅里，乐队奏得震天响，喇叭齐鸣，人声鼎沸。
「行了，」里科说，「掏枪，托尼。我去撬里面的柜子。」

===Original===
“God,” said Tony, “it’ll take too long.”
Rico looked at him. Tony, holding the sacks in one arm, pulled out his gun. Rico walked over to the manager.
“Listen,” he said, “I want action. Go in and open that box and slip me the jack. One funny move and I’ll blow your guts out.”
“Oh, my God!” cried the Czech.
They disappeared. There was a dead silence in the lobby. One of the check girls began to cry.
“Nice little holdup,” said Joe.
===Chinese===
「天哪，」托尼说，「那太费时间了。」
里科看着他。托尼一条胳膊夹着袋子，拔出了枪。里科走向经理。
「听着，」他说，「我要你动起来。进去把柜子打开，把钱递给我。敢耍一下花招，我轰烂你的肠子。」
「噢，我的天！」捷克人喊道。
他们消失了。大堂里死一般寂静。一个衣帽女郎哭了起来。
「漂亮的小劫案。」乔说。

===Original===
Nobody said anything.
“Yeah,” said Joe nonchalant, “fine little holdup.”
He smiled at the waiter, who looked hastily away and turned agonized eyes on Tony as if to say: “Look, I can’t help what that bird’s saying.”
Two more men came in the street door and were backed up against the wall by Otero. The seconds seemed like hours to Tony, who was slowly losing his nerve.
The manager reappeared, followed by Rico, who had his gun pressed against the manager’s back. Rico’s pockets bulged.
“Good Lord,” hissed Tony, “let’s go.”
===Chinese===
没人说话。
「是啊，」乔漫不经心地说，「精彩的小劫案。」
他朝侍者笑了笑，侍者慌忙把脸扭开，把痛苦的眼神转向托尼，仿佛在说：「瞧，那家伙说什么我可管不着。」
又有两个男人从临街的门进来，被奥特罗逼到墙根。对托尼来说一秒像一个钟头，他的胆气正在一点点泄掉。
经理又出现了，里科跟在后面，枪顶着经理的后背。里科的口袋鼓鼓囊囊。
「老天爷，」托尼咬着牙低声说，「走吧。」

===Original===
Three men and two women came out into the lobby from the club proper. They stopped, petrified.
The strain was beginning to tell on Rico, whose face was ghastly.
“Stick up your hands, you,” he cried, “and don’t move.”
Two of the men and both of the women put up their hands, but the third man, burly and red-faced, hesitated.
“Good God,” said Joe, “it’s Courtney, the bull.”
Joe’s mask of nonchalance slipped from him instantly; he dropped his hands and reached for his gun.
===Chinese===
三男两女从俱乐部正厅走进大堂。他们站住了，呆若木鸡。
紧张开始显现在里科身上，他的脸色白得吓人。
「举起手来，你们这些家伙，」他喊道，「不许动。」
两个男人和两个女人都举起了手，只有第三个男人，魁梧红脸，迟疑着。
「我的天，」乔说，「是考特尼（Courtney），条子。」
乔那副漫不经心的假面顿时掉了；他放下手，去摸枪。

===Original===
“Beat it,” cried Rico to Tony and Otero.
They made a break for the door. One of the women with Courtney fainted and fell hard, hitting her head.
“Don’t touch her,” cried Rico, “my finger’s itching.”
Joe followed the others, backing out with his gun in his hand. Courtney’s face was purple. He glanced at his wife, lying pale and unconscious on the floor, then, shouting “you dirty bums” reached for his gun. Rico fired. Courtney took two steps towards Rico, staring. Then he fell heavily, his arms spread.
At the door Rico collided with a drunken man, who was just entering. The man tried to hug him, but he knocked him down with a blow of his fist.
Rico jumped on the running-board and bellowed:
===Chinese===
「撤。」里科朝托尼和奥特罗喊道。
他们夺门而逃。跟考特尼一起的一个女人晕倒，重重摔在地上，磕了头。
「别碰她，」里科喊道，「我的手指正痒。」
乔倒退着跟其他人往外撤，手里握着枪。考特尼的脸涨成紫色。他瞥了一眼躺在地板上苍白昏迷的妻子，然后骂着「你们这帮脏货」去摸枪。里科开枪了。考特尼朝里科迈了两步，眼睛瞪着。然后他沉重地栽倒，双臂张开。
门口里科撞上一个正要进来的醉汉。那人想搂他，他一拳把那人打倒。
里科跳上踏脚板，吼道：

===Original===
“Open her up, Tony. This ain’t no picnic.”
Tony was unnerved and tears were dripping down onto his hands. Joe and Otero sat silent in the back seat. Otero rolled a cigarette between his palms. Nobody said anything.
Tony took a corner, careening. The wind had died down a little and it had begun to snow again, a thin, cold, powdery snow. The whistles were still blowing, but fainter now, one leaving off, then another.
“Well,” said Rico, “I plugged him.”
“Yeah,” said Joe, “I seen him fall. Like a ton of bricks.”
“Well,” said Otero, “what can you do? The fool, pulling a gat!”
===Chinese===
「开快点儿，托尼。这可不是闹着玩。」
托尼垮了，泪水一滴滴落在手上。乔和奥特罗坐在后座不吭声。奥特罗在掌间搓着一支烟。谁都没说话。
托尼拐过一个街角，车身歪斜。风小了些，又下起雪来，细小干冷的粉雪。哨子还在响，但弱了，一声停了，又一声。
「行了，」里科说，「我打中他了。」
「嗯，」乔说，「我看见他倒了。像座塔塌下来。」
「唉，」奥特罗说，「有什么法子？那蠢货，偏要拔枪！」

===Original===
Tony said nothing, but sat with his eyes fixed.
“It’s our hips for this,” said Joe.
Otero shrugged and lit a cigarette.
“Losing your guts, Joe?” asked Rico.
“Me!” said Joe.
Tony turned into the alleyway back of The Palermo. Rico put the sacks under his coat and jumped out. Otero and Joe followed him.
===Chinese===
托尼没说话，直挺挺坐着，眼睛发直。
「这回咱们要掉脑袋了。」乔说。
奥特罗耸耸肩，点上烟。
「泄气了，乔？」里科问。
「我！」乔说。
托尼拐进帕勒莫（The Palermo）后身的巷子。里科把袋子掖进大衣，跳下车。奥特罗和乔跟着他。

===Original===
“Tony,” said Rico, “ditch that can, then come back for your split. Hear what I say. Ditch it good and proper. We’ll wait.”
“Look,” said Joe, “I got to have my split now. I’m on at one-twenty. Boy, I can’t miss that turn.”
“OK,” said Rico.
Tony drove off down the alley. Rico knocked at the door and Carillo let them in.
===Chinese===
「托尼，」里科说，「把车处理掉，再回来拿你那份。记住我的话。处理干净利落。我们等着。」
「听着，」乔说，「我现在就得拿我那份。一点二十我要上场。老兄，那一场我不能误。」
「行。」里科说。
托尼顺着巷子把车开走了。里科敲敲门，卡里洛放他们进去。

### II / 二

===Original===
When they came in Vettori was standing in the middle of the room mopping his forehead with his big white silk handkerchief. Beads of sweat stood out all over his swarthy, fat face.
Rico threw the sacks on the table and began to empty his pockets.
“There’s the dough,” said Rico, “looks like a good haul.”
Joe sat down at the table under the green-shaded lamp without taking off his hat or coat. Otero took the riot gun from under his coat and locked it up in a cupboard. Vettori knew there was something the matter. His eyes narrowed.
“Well,” he said again.
“Everything was OK,” said Rico, “only I had to plug a guy.”
===Chinese===
他们进来时，维托里正站在屋子当中，用那块白绸大手帕擦额头。黝黑的胖脸上全是汗珠。
里科把袋子扔在桌上，开始往外掏口袋。
「钱在这儿，」里科说，「看样子收获不小。」
乔在绿灯罩台灯下的桌边坐下，帽子大衣都没脱。奥特罗从大衣底下取出霰弹枪，锁进柜子里。维托里知道出了事。他的眼睛眯了起来。
「怎么样。」他又说了一遍。
「一切都顺，」里科说，「只是我不得不撂倒一个家伙。」

===Original===
Vettori fell down into a chair and stared out the window.
“Yeah,” said Joe, trying to smile, “and the guy was Courtney.”
Vettori put his head on the back of his chair and stared at the ceiling. Then he sat up suddenly and banged on the table with both fists.
“Goddamn!” he cried, “what did I tell you, Rico! What did I tell you! Love of God, didn’t I tell you no gunwork?”
Rico was white with rage.
“Listen, Sam, you think I’m gonna let a guy pull a gat on me. What the hell! Any more of them cracks and this is my last job.” Vettori made an elaborate, tragic gesture.
===Chinese===
维托里跌坐进椅子里，望着窗外发怔。
「是啊，」乔强笑着说，「而且那家伙是考特尼。」
维托里把头靠在椅背上，两眼盯着天花板。然后他猛地坐直，双拳砸在桌上。
「该死！」他嚷道，「我怎么跟你说的，里科！我怎么说的！看在老天爷份上，我没说别开枪吗？」
里科气得发白。
「听着，萨姆，你以为我会让一个家伙拿枪指着我？见鬼！再说这种混账话，这就是我最后一票。」维托里做了一个夸张的、悲剧性的手势。

===Original===
“Yeah, you bet this is your last job.”
Joe took of his derby and put it beside him on the table. His face was dead white.
“You said it,” said Joe. “They’ll get us sure for this.”
Vettori shook his big head slowly from side to side.
“They’ll get us dead sure for this.”
Rico began to comb his hair.
===Chinese===
「是啊，这保准是你的最后一票。」
乔摘下圆顶礼帽，放在桌上自己旁边。他的脸死白。
「说得对，」乔说。「这回他们准会抓到我们。」
维托里的大脑袋缓缓地左右直摇。
「这回他们铁定会抓到我们。」
里科梳起头发来。

===Original===
“Maybe you better go over and give yourself up,” he said; then dropping his sarcastic tone, “listen, how the hell they gonna get us? Why, you’re the finest bunch of yellow bastards I ever seen.”
“Not me,” said Otero.
Joe tried to smile.
“Wait till you see the papers.”
Rico came over and leaned on the table.
“Listen, don’t they always play that stuff up in the papers. Courtney’s the only guy in the place that ever seen one of us before. Come on, snap out of it. And split the dough.”
===Chinese===
「兴许你们最好过去自首。」他说；随即收起讥讽的口气，「听着，他们他妈怎么抓我们？得，你们是我见过最够格的一帮怂包软蛋。」
「我不怂。」奥特罗说。
乔试着笑了笑。
「等着瞧报纸吧。」
里科走过来，俯身撑着桌子。
「听着，报纸不老是拿这种事炒个够？那地方只有考特尼一个人从前见过我们中的一个。行了，振作点。分钱。」

===Original===
But Vettori sat inert, mopping his face. Suddenly he asked:
“Where’s Tony?”
“He’s ditching the can,” said Rico.
“Suppose they pick him up?”
Rico began to open the sacks.
“That’ll be just too bad,” said Joe.
===Chinese===
可维托里呆坐着，擦着脸。突然他问：
「托尼呢？」
「处理车去了。」里科说。
「要是他们截住他呢？」
里科开始解袋子。
「那就糟糕透了。」乔说。

===Original===
Rico laughed.
“A fine bunch of yeggs!”
Vettori got to his feet in a fury.
“You, Rico! Shut your mouth. You think I want to hang because you get yellow and shoot somebody.”
Rico, very calm, put his hand in his pocket and said:
“Sam, you get funny with me and you won’t get no split at all. Only a horseshoe wreath.”
===Chinese===
里科笑了。
「一伙够格的强盗！」
维托里勃然大怒，站了起来。
「你，里科！闭上你的嘴。你以为我愿意因为你们吓破了胆、开枪打了人，就跟着去上绞架？」
里科非常平静，把手插进口袋，说：
「萨姆，你再跟我来劲，你连一份都分不着。只能分到一个马蹄形花圈。」

===Original===
“Oh, hell, Sam,” said Joe, “we’re all in it, ain’t we? Come on, split the dough.”
Vettori sat down. Otero stood a little behind him, watching.
“Since you want it, Sam,” said Rico, his face pale and drawn, “you’re gonna get it. Listen, you split even, that’s all. Hear me! You get an even split.”
Vettori said nothing. Joe sat rigid, ready to dive under the table. For months Scabby had been predicting this break; now it had come. Joe feared Vettori and Rico equally, but something told him that Rico would win.
Vettori let his hands fall on the table.
“All right, Rico,” he said, “I split even. Sit down and we’ll divvy.”
===Chinese===
「唉，得了，萨姆，」乔说，「咱们都在一条船上，不是吗？行了，分钱吧。」
维托里坐下了。奥特罗站在他身后一点的地方看着。
「既然你要这样，萨姆，」里科说，脸苍白而绷紧，「那就这样。听着，你均分，就这样。听见没！你均分。」
维托里没说话。乔僵直地坐着，准备随时钻到桌子底下。几个月来斯卡比一直在预言这场决裂；现在它来了。乔对维托里和里科同样害怕，但有什么告诉他，赢的会是里科。
维托里把双手落在桌上。
「行，里科，」他说，「我均分，规规矩矩地均分。」

===Original===
But Rico didn’t move.
“You got a gun on you, Sam?” he asked.
Vettori looked up at him.
“Sure I got a gun on me.”
“Well, don’t try to use it.”
“No,” said Otero, “don’t try to use it.”
===Chinese===
可里科没动。
「你身上带枪了吗，萨姆？」他问。
维托里抬头看他。
「当然带了。」
「那就别想用它。」
「对，」奥特罗说，「别想用它。」

===Original===
Vettori’s face went slack. He sat tapping on the table with his fat fingers.
“Rico,” he said finally, “I split even on the square.”
Rico’s victory was complete. Joe looked at him with admiration. Sam was a tough bird, but Rico was tougher.
Vettori got up, walked across the room and stood looking out the window.
===Chinese===
维托里的脸松垮下来。他坐着，用胖手指敲桌子。
「里科，」他终于开口，「我规规矩矩均分。」
里科的胜利圆满了。乔带着钦佩看他。萨姆是只硬鸟，可里科更硬。
维托里起身，穿过房间，站在窗前望出去。

### III / 三

===Original===
Joe handed Rico a sheet of paper full of figures. Rico read: 9,331.75.
“All right,” said Rico, “split it five ways and we’ll make up Scabby’s split between us.”
Otero sat with his chair tipped back against the wall, smoking a cigarette with his eyes closed. Vettori was playing solitaire and swearing softly to himself.
Joe looked at his watch.
“Quarter till. I got to beat it. Say, Sam, call Carillo and let him get me a cab, will you?”
Sam heaved himself to his feet and called Carillo. In a moment the bouncer put his flattened face in the door.
===Chinese===
乔递给里科一张写满数字的纸。里科念道：9,331.75。
「好，」里科说，「分五份，斯卡比那份咱们几个摊。」
奥特罗椅子往后翘着靠墙坐着，闭着眼抽烟。维托里在玩单人纸牌，轻声咒骂着自己。
乔看了看表。
「差一刻了。我得走了。哎，萨姆，叫卡里洛给我叫辆车，行吗？」
萨姆撑着站起来，喊了卡里洛。不一会儿，看场子的把那张扁脸探进门来。

===Original===
“Three dicks downstairs, boss.”
“Who are they?” asked Vettori.
“Flaherty and two guys I don’t know, boss. They want to see you.”
Vettori stood looking at the floor. Carillo jumped in and shut the door.
“Christ,” he said, “they’re coming up.”
Rico leapt to his feet, ran across the room and opened a panel in the wall.
===Chinese===
「楼下有三个警探，老板。」
「谁？」维托里问。
「弗莱厄蒂（Flaherty），还有两个我不认识的，老板。他们要见你。」
维托里站着，眼睛看地板。卡里洛闪身进来，关上门。
「天哪，」他说，「他们上来了。」
里科跳起来，跑过房间，打开墙上的一块暗板。

===Original===
“Come on, Joe,” he said, “you can slip out the back way. Stay where you are, Otero, and go right on smoking. Send Joe’s cab around in the alley, Bat.”
Vettori looked at Rico.
“You suppose they know something, Rico?”
“Not unless they picked Tony up. You don’t know nothing, Sam, see? I’ll be right here listening, and if there’s any trouble, why, it’ll be tough on the dicks.”
Vettori scooped up the money, wrapped his coat around it, and handed it to Rico. Joe went through the panel, followed by Rico. There was a knock at the door.
Vettori nodded and Carillo opened the door. Two plainclothes men stepped in and stood looking around the room. One was tall and burly in a huge ulster; the other was short and very young. They both had their right hands in their overcoat pockets.
===Chinese===
「来，乔，」他说，「你从后路溜出去。待着别动，奥特罗，接着抽你的烟。把乔的车打发到巷子里等着，巴特（Bat）。」
维托里看着里科。
「你觉得他们摸到什么了吗，里科？」
「除非他们截住了托尼。你什么都不知道，萨姆，明白？我就在这儿听着，真有麻烦，哼，那几个警探有得好受。」
维托里把钱拢起来，用大衣裹住，递给里科。乔钻进暗板，里科跟着。门上响起敲门声。
维托里点点头，卡里洛开了门。两个便衣迈进来，站在那儿打量屋子。一个高大粗壮，裹着件肥大的粗呢大衣；另一个矮个儿，非常年轻。两人右手都插在大衣口袋里。

===Original===
“All right, Carillo,” said Vettori, “go ahead. That’s all.”
“Wait a minute,” said the burly one, “tell Flaherty we’ll be down in a couple of minutes, for him to wait.”
“Sure, sure,” said Carillo.
He went out closing the door softly.
“Well,” said Vettori, “you want to see me?”
“Yeah,” said the burly one, who did all the talking, “we want to see you, Vettori.”
===Chinese===
「行了，卡里洛，」维托里说，「去吧。没你的事了。」
「等一下，」粗壮的那个说，「告诉弗莱厄蒂我们几分钟后下去，让他等着。」
「行，行。」卡里洛说。
他出去，轻轻带上门。
「怎么样，」维托里说，「要见我？」
「对，」粗壮的那个说，话全是他一人讲，「我们要见你，维托里。」

===Original===
“Well, here I am!”
Otero opened his eyes long enough to look at them, then closed them again and went on smoking.
“Vettori,” said the detective, “we want some information.”
“Well?”
Vettori sat down at the table and began to shuffle the cards.
“There’s a big Cadillac draped around a pole a couple of blocks down the street, and we just wondered if you knew anything about it.”
===Chinese===
「行，我不就在这儿！」
奥特罗睁开眼看了他们一眼，又闭上，接着抽烟。
「维托里，」警探说，「我们想要点情况。」
「说。」
维托里在桌边坐下，开始洗牌。
「隔两条街有辆大凯迪拉克缠在电线杆上，我们就想问问你知不知道点什么。」

===Original===
Vettori began to lay out a game of solitaire.
“How should I know anything about it? Ain’t it got no licence plates on it?”
“Sure, but they’re phoney.”
“Yeah?”
“Yeah. It was stolen about eight o’clock tonight on the North Side and we got a pretty good description of the guy that stole it.”
“Well,” said Vettori, “I got a good business. What the hell’d I be doing stealing automobiles.”
===Chinese===
维托里开始摆单人牌局。
「我怎么会知道？车上没车牌吗？」
「有，可是是假的。」
「是吗？」
「是。今晚八点左右在城北被人偷的，偷车的家伙长什么样，我们掌握得相当清楚。」
「唉，」维托里说，「我有正经买卖。偷汽车干什么。」

===Original===
He laughed and shook his head.
“Oh, you got me wrong,” said the detective with elaborate innocence. “You see, it’s piled up right straight down the street from here and I thought maybe it was some of the guys from your joint, see? I mean some of the young guys that come here to dance.”
“Well,” said Vettori, “how would I know?”
The detective took out a cigar and began to chew on it.
“Wasn’t there nobody in it?” asked Otero.
“Yeah,” said the detective, “one guy. But he beat it.”
===Chinese===
他笑着摇了摇头。
「噢，你误会了，」警探说，装出一副全然无辜的样子。「是这样，车就堆在这条街直过去不远的地方，我想也许是你这场子里的人干的，明白吗？我是说那些来跳舞的年轻人。」
「唉，」维托里说，「我怎么知道？」
警探掏出一支雪茄，嚼了起来。
「车里没人吗？」奥特罗问。
「有，」警探说，「一个人。不过跑了。」

===Original===
“I don’t know nothing about it,” said Vettori.
“Well, no harm in asking,” said the detective. “Come on, Mike, let’s get going. I guess Vettori don’t know nothing about it.”
The two of them walked slowly to the door. The big one turned.
“Say, Vettori,” he said, “did you hear the news?”
Vettori looked up.
“What news?”
===Chinese===
「这事我一概不知。」维托里说。
「唉，问问总没坏处。」警探说。「走吧，迈克（Mike）。我看维托里是一概不知了。」
两人慢慢走到门口。大个子回过头。
「哎，维托里，」他说，「听到消息没有？」
维托里抬起头。
「什么消息？」

===Original===
“Why, some bastard bumped Cap. Courtney off over at the Casa Alvarado.”
“Yeah?” said Vettori, “some guys are sure careless with the lead. That’s a tough break.”
The young detective opened the door and they started out.
“Ain’t it?” said the big one. “Well, so long.”
As soon as the door closed, Vettori went over and shot the bolt, then peeped out through the shutter. Rico came out of his hiding place.
“Well,” said Vettori, glancing at Rico, “things ain’t going so good.”
===Chinese===
「嗐，哪个混蛋在卡萨·阿尔瓦拉多把考特尼队长给崩了。」
「是吗？」维托里说，「有些人放铅弹可真不小心。真是不走运。」
年轻警探开了门，两人往外走。
「可不是。」大个子说。「行了，回见。」
门一关上，维托里就走过去闩上门闩，然后从百叶窗缝往外窥视。里科从藏身处出来了。
「唉，」维托里说着，瞥了里科一眼，「事情不妙啊。」

===Original===
Rico shrugged.
“They don’t know nothing. Just feeling around. Listen, Sam, where’s your guts? We got to stick together on this.”
“I know,” said Vettori, falling back into his chair, “but I never seen things break so tough.”
Rico held out a roll of bills.
“Here’s your split, Sam.”
Vettori took the bills and stuffed them into his pocket. Rico handed Otero his. Otero got up and put on his overcoat.
===Chinese===
里科耸耸肩。
「他们什么都不知道。瞎摸而已。听着，萨姆，你的胆子呢？这事上咱们得抱成团。」
「我知道，」维托里说，跌回椅子里，「可我从没见过世道崩得这么凶。」
里科递过来一卷钞票。
「这是你那份，萨姆。」
维托里接过钞票，塞进口袋。里科把奥特罗那份递给他。奥特罗起身穿上大衣。

===Original===
“I think I go see my woman,” he said.
When he had gone, Rico went over and sat down beside Vettori.
“Listen, Sam,” he said, “I been taking orders too long. We’re done. Get the idea? But we got to see this through. We get a break and we’ll come clean. Only we got to shoot straight. See what I mean? I got a rope around my neck right now and they can only hang you once. If anybody gets yellow and squeals, my gun’s gonna speak its piece.”
“That’s OK with me,” said Sam.
They sat silent. Downstairs the jazz band was playing and the saxophone was sending vibrations along the floor. Vettori laid out another game of solitaire.
“Funny for Tony to crash,” he said.
===Chinese===
「我看我去看我的女人了。」他说。
他走后，里科过去在维托里身边坐下。
「听着，萨姆，」他说，「听人差遣的日子我过够了。咱们之间完了。明白吗？可这摊事得兜到底。捱过这一关，咱们就两清。只是得走直线。懂我意思？我这脖子上已经套着绞索了，绞刑也只能绞一回。谁要是怂了乱咬，我的枪会替它把话说完。」
「我没意见。」萨姆说。
两人坐着不说话。楼下爵士乐队还在演奏，萨克斯管还在把震颤顺着地板送上来。维托里又摆开一局单人牌。
「托尼居然撞了车，怪事。」他说。

===Original===
“He lost his nerve,” said Rico.
“You suppose he’ll show?”
“Not till tomorrow if he’s got any sense. I’ll leave his split with you.”
===Chinese===
「他没了胆气。」里科说。
「你说他会露面吗？」
「他要还有点脑子，明天之前不会来。他那份我留给你。」

### IV / 四

===Original===
Rico went over to see Ma Magdalena, the fence. Her fruit store was still open and her son Arrigo was sitting half-asleep beside a pile of oranges.
“Hello,” he said.
“Where’s Ma?” asked Rico.
Arrigo pulled a cord which rang a bell in the rooms beyond the store. Ma, leaning on her stick, came out into the store. Seeing Rico, she said:
“Oh, it’s you! Well, well! Come back. Come back.”
“Can I come too, Ma?” said Arrigo.
===Chinese===
里科去看销赃人马格达莱娜大妈（Ma Magdalena）。她的水果店还开着，她儿子阿里戈（Arrigo）坐在一堆橘子旁边，半睡半醒。
「你好。」他说。
「大妈呢？」里科问。
阿里戈拉了拉绳，店后屋里响起铃。大妈拄着手杖出来，走进店堂。看见里科，她说：
「哟，是你啊！好好！进来。进来。」
「我也来吗，妈？」阿里戈说。

===Original===
“You stay and mind the store, you lazy loafer,” said Ma, shaking her stick at him.
Arrigo sat down once more by the pile of oranges.
Rico followed Ma Magdalena back into her little office. She pulled up a chair for him and he sat down, then she got out a bottle.
“You talk, I drink,” she said, sitting down beside him and pouring herself a drink.
Rico took out his split, peeled off a few bills and handed her the rest.
“Plant it,” he said.
===Chinese===
「你留下看店，你这个懒汉。」大妈说着，朝他晃手杖。
阿里戈重新在橘子堆边坐下。
里科跟着马格达莱娜大妈进了她的小里间。她给他拖过一把椅子，他坐下，然后她拿出一瓶酒。
「你说，我喝。」她说着在他身边坐下，给自己倒了一杯。
里科掏出他那份，抽掉几张钞票，把余下的递给她。
「藏起来。」他说。

===Original===
She took the roll, counted it, and put it down inside her dress.
“Had a big New Year’s Eve, did you?”
“Yeah,” said Rico, “plenty big. There’ll be lots of fun tomorrow.”
“Well, well,” said Ma, “that’s the way it goes.”
She poured herself another glass of wine, then she reached over and touched Rico with her stick.
“Look, Rico, you ain’t got a nice little girl who wants a big diamond ring, have you?”
===Chinese===
她接过那卷钱，数了数，塞进怀里。
「除夕夜干了一大票，是吧？」
「嗯，」里科说，「不小。明天有得热闹了。」
「好好，」大妈说，「世道就是这样。」
她又给自己倒了一杯酒，然后探过身，用手杖碰了碰里科。
「我说，里科，你就没有一个想要大钻戒的俊俏小姑娘吗？」

===Original===
“Me, buy a diamond ring for a skirt?”
Ma Magdalena made a clucking noise and shook her head.
“You are cold, Rico. Don’t like wine. Don’t like women. You are no good, Rico.”
Rico smiled.
“Me, I like women once in a while, but I ain’t putting out no diamond rings.”
Leaving Ma Magdalena’s, Rico went in the direction of Sicily Pete’s.
===Chinese===
「我，给娘们儿买钻戒？」
马格达莱娜大妈咂了咂嘴，摇了摇头。
「你这个人冷，里科。不喜欢酒。不喜欢女人。你不行啊，里科。」
里科笑了。
「我嘛，偶尔也喜欢女人，可钻戒我是不掏的。」
离开马格达莱娜大妈那儿，里科朝西西里人皮特的店走去。

===Original===
At Sicily Pete’s the mechanical piano was playing. Three men, all Italians, and two girls, both Americans, were sitting at a front table. They were drunk. They played with their food, spilled their coffee, and banged on the plates with their knives. Pete stood behind the counter, scowling.
When Rico came in he said:
“Hello, my friend, where have you been keeping yourself?”
“I haven’t been around lately. Got some noisy birds, ain’t you?” Pete shrugged his shoulders.
“Yes, the fools. They drink gin. That is no drink for an Italian.” Rico took out his cigarettes and offered Pete one. They stood smoking. One of the girls pulled up her dress and fixed her garter. Rico smiled.
“Get an eyeful of that, Pete.”
===Chinese===
西西里人皮特店里，自动钢琴正响着。三个男人，全是意大利人，和两个姑娘，都是美国人，坐在前面一张桌边。他们喝醉了。把食物拨来拨去，咖啡洒了，还用刀子敲盘子。皮特站在柜台后面，沉着脸。
里科进来时他说：
「你好啊，我的朋友，这些日子躲哪儿去了？」
「近来没在附近转。店里有点吵人的鸟，是吧？」皮特耸了耸肩。
「是啊，这帮蠢货。他们喝杜松子酒。那不是意大利人喝的东西。」里科掏出烟，递给皮特一支。两人站着抽烟。一个姑娘撩起裙子整理吊袜带。里科笑了。
「饱饱眼福，皮特。」

===Original===
“Yes, yes,” said Pete, “that’s all I get, an eyeful. Every night I stand here while other people have a good time.”
The girl looked up at Rico and he winked at her. She said to one of the men:
“Look at that smarty over there. He thinks he’s cute.”
The man looked foggily at Rico. Pete put his hand on Rico’s arm.
“My friend, don’t start no trouble, please. That’s all we have around here, trouble. With one thing and another, I think I go back to Italy.”
Rico turned his back on the girl.
===Chinese===
「是啊，是啊，」皮特说，「我也就捞得着眼福。每晚上我都站在这儿，看别人寻欢作乐。」
那姑娘抬眼看里科，他朝她挤了挤眼。她对其中一个男人说：
「瞧那边那个自作聪明的。他还以为自己俏呢。」
那男人迷迷糊糊地看向里科。皮特把手搭在里科胳膊上。
「我的朋友，请别惹麻烦。这附近别的没有，麻烦管够。这样那样的事一多，我看我还是回意大利去。」
里科转过身，不再理那姑娘。

===Original===
“OK,” he said.
While Pete was getting Rico a cup of coffee, a newsboy came in:
“**Extra! Extra!** All about the big holdup.”
Rico bought a paper and glanced at the three-inch headlines.
===Chinese===
「行。」他说。
皮特给里科端咖啡的时候，一个报童跑进来：
「**号外！号外！**大劫案全情。」
里科买了份报，扫了一眼三英寸高的大标题。

===Original===
> Thugs kill Captain Courtney 

 in Casa Alvarado holdup
===Chinese===
> 暴徒杀死考特尼队长

 于卡萨·阿尔瓦拉多劫案中

===Original===
Rico showed Pete the paper.
“Another killing,” he said.
“Yes,” said Pete, “kill, kill, that’s all they do. I wish to God I was back in Sicily. The Mafia, what is that? That is a kindergarten.”
One of the Italians bought a paper and started to read the account of the holdup aloud. All the people round the table stopped eating to listen. Rico sipped his coffee and watched them.
===Chinese===
里科把报纸递给皮特看。
「又一起凶杀。」他说。
「是啊，」皮特说，「杀啊，杀啊，他们就干这个。我求老天爷让我回西西里。黑手党算什么？那不过是幼儿园。」
一个意大利人买了份报，大声念起劫案的报道。桌边的人都停下吃的，听着。里科啜着咖啡，看着他们。

### V / 五

===Original===
Tony hadn’t slept all night. He lay in the cold dark room, sweating. The covers felt as heavy as lead and from time to time he tossed them off, only to pull them over him again as the lake wind, streaming in the window, made him shiver. At intervals he would fall into a doze. Then he would see a windy street, feel a car skidding under him, feel a sickening jolt. He would wake with a start and sit up in bed.
“They’ll get us for this,” he kept repeating, “they’ll get us sure.”
Tony smoked cigarette after cigarette. In his despair he cast about for someone to put the blame on. It was all Midge’s fault. Wasn’t she always after him to make more jack so she could put on the dog? Hadn’t he tried to go straight and drive a taxi and make an honest living? Yeah, and hadn’t Sam Vettori and Rico offered him money to quit his job and give them a lift on their stickups? Well, you couldn’t quit a gang; once you were in, you were in!
Tony sat up in bed and looked out across the roofs outside his window. The sun was coming up and a cold, windy winter morning was dawning. Suddenly, he began to feel sick at his stomach. He lay down, but that didn’t help him, then he tossed from side to side.
Tony had to vomit. He jumped out of bed and ran for the bathroom. When he came out his mother was lighting the stove. She acted as if she didn’t see him. He went back to his room but stopped in the doorway.
“Hello, mom,” he said.
===Chinese===
托尼一夜没睡。他躺在又冷又黑的屋里，出着汗。被子沉得像铅，他不时把它掀掉，可湖风从窗口灌进来，冻得他发抖，又只好再拉回身上。他时不时坠入浅睡。于是就会看见一条刮风的街，感觉身下车子打滑，一阵令人作呕的猛震。他会惊醒，从床上坐起来。
「这回他们要抓我们了，」他翻来覆去地说，「准会抓到我们。」
托尼一支接一支地抽烟。绝望中他急着找个人把罪责推上去。全怪米琪（Midge）。不是她老撺掇他多弄钱，好让她摆阔吗？他不是试过走正道，开出租车，挣干干净净的钱吗？是啊，萨姆·维托里和里科不是拿钱劝他辞了活儿，让他们抢劫时搭把手吗？唉，帮派是退不出的；一旦入了伙，就断了退路！
托尼坐起身，望向窗外一片屋顶的上方。太阳正在升起，一个寒冷多风的冬晨破晓了。突然，他开始觉得胃里难受。他躺下，可没用，于是翻来覆去。
托尼忍不住要吐。他跳下床，奔向卫生间。他出来时，母亲正在生炉子。她装作没看见他。他回自己房间去，却在门口停住了。
「你好，妈。」他说。

===Original===
His mother paid no attention.
“Say,” said Tony, “what’s the matter?”
His mother turned and with her hands on her hips stared at him.
“Go back to bed, you loafer,” she said, “I am sick of you. You are no good on earth. Just like your father.”
“Aw, mom,” said Tony.
“Don’t try to salve me,” said Tony’s mother. “You go back to bed and get sober. You think I don’t know nothing, don’t you? Just like your father.”
===Chinese===
母亲不理睬。
「哎，」托尼说，「怎么了？」
母亲转过身，双手叉腰，瞪着他。
「回床上去，你这个二流子，」她说，「我烦透你了。你在这世上一点用没有。跟你爹一个样。」
「唉，妈。」托尼说。
「别想哄我。」托尼的母亲说。「你回床上去，醒醒酒。你以为我什么都不知道，是不是？跟你爹一个样。」

===Original===
“I’m not drunk,” said Tony, “I’m sick.”
His mother turned her back and went on with her cooking. Tony went into his room, slammed the door, and got back into bed. A deadly depression settled on him. The world looked black.
He heard his mother go out, then he got up, dressed and made himself some toast and coffee. Anyway, he wanted his split.
On the way to Vettori’s he met Father McConagha. The priest was a big man with a big, pale face. He walked with a rolling gait and there was something arrogant about him. Tony took off his hat.
“Good morning, Father.”
“Good morning, Antonio,” said Father McConagha. “Where have you been, my boy? I haven’t seen you for months.”
===Chinese===
「我没醉，」托尼说，「我是病了。」
母亲转过身去，接着做她的饭。托尼进了自己房间，砰地关门，回到床上。一阵要命的消沉压住了他。世界一片黑。
他听见母亲出去了，便起来穿好衣服，给自己烤了面包、煮了咖啡。不管怎么说，他那份钱要拿。
去维托里那儿的路上，他遇见了麦科纳加神父（Father McConagha）。神父是个大块头，一张大白脸。走起路来摇摇摆摆，带着股傲气。托尼摘下帽子。
「早上好，神父。」
「早上好，安东尼奥。」麦科纳加神父说。「这些日子你去哪儿了，我的孩子？我好几个月没见你了。」

===Original===
“I been working,” said Tony.
“What sort of work?” asked Father McConagha, putting his hand on Tony’s shoulder.
“I been driving a taxi.”
The priest nodded his head slowly.
“That is good work, Antonio.”
Tony couldn’t look at Father McConagha and kept twisting his hat in his hands and staring at it. Father McConagha talked to him for a minute or two about the rewards of honesty and the happiness to be derived from doing your work faithfully, then he said:
===Chinese===
「在干活。」托尼说。
「干什么活？」麦科纳加神父问，把手搭在托尼肩上。
「开出租车。」
神父缓缓点头。
「那是好活儿，安东尼奥。」
托尼不敢看麦科纳加神父，手里绞着帽子，眼睛盯着它。麦科纳加神父跟他谈了一两分钟诚实的好处、本分做事能得到的幸福，然后说：

===Original===
“Antonio, one day your father asked me to look out for you. Your father was a good man, but weak. Remember this, Antonio, if you are ever in any trouble I am the one to come to.”
Tony flushed and said:
“Thank you, Father.”
When Father McConagha had gone, Tony began to speculate. Did he know anything? Why, on this very morning, had he said something about being in trouble? Tony respected and admired Father McConagha. He felt that he could always turn to him.
Talking with the priest had made him feel stronger, but now that the priest had gone all the hopelessness of the night before rushed back on him. He took out a cigarette and lit it with shaking hands.
“They’ll get us sure for this,” he said.
===Chinese===
「安东尼奥，当年你父亲托我照看你。你父亲是个好人，可软弱。记住这个，安东尼奥，你要是遇到什么难处，来找的就是我。」
托尼脸红了，说：
「谢谢您，神父。」
麦科纳加神父走后，托尼犯起了嘀咕。他知道些什么吗？怎么偏偏在今天早上，说起遇到难处的话？托尼敬重也佩服麦科纳加神父。他觉得自己永远可以投奔他。
跟神父说话让他觉得硬朗了些，可神父一走，头天夜里那种万念俱灰又涌了回来。他掏出一支烟，用发抖的手点上。
「这回他们准会抓到我们。」他说。

### VI / 六

===Original===
Seal Skin couldn’t get Otero sober. She made him eat tomatoes and she gave him a cold bath, but nothing seemed to do him any good. He walked about the flat in his underclothes singing songs in Bastard Spanish and bragging about what a great brave man he was. Only one man in the world braver: Rico.
Seal Skin was dead for sleep, but she didn’t shut her eyes for fear Otero would do some crazy thing like shooting out the window at the street light (he had done this one night) or going out in his underclothes.
Otero sat at the table with his automatic beside him, singing at the top of his voice.
===Chinese===
海豹皮没法让奥特罗醒酒。她让他吃西红柿，又给他冲了个冷水澡，可什么都没用。他穿着内衣在公寓里走来走去，用蹩脚的西班牙语唱着歌，吹嘘自己是多了不起的勇士。世上只有一个比他更勇的：里科。
海豹皮困得要死，却不敢合眼，生怕奥特罗干出什么疯事来，比如朝窗外的路灯开枪（有天夜里他就干过），或者穿着内衣就上街。
奥特罗坐在桌边，自动手枪搁在手边，扯着嗓子唱。
===Original===
“Look,” he cried, “I am Ramón Otero, a great brave man. I ain’t afraid of nobody or nothing. I can drink any man in the world under the table and I can outshoot any man that walks on two legs. Only Rico; he is my friend. He is a great man like Pancho Villa and I love him with a great love. I would not shoot Rico if he shot me first. Rico is my friend and I love him with a great love.”
Then he got up and, snapping his fingers, began to dance, stamping with his heels, wiggling his hips, till Seal Skin nearly fell out of her chair laughing.
Towards morning he went to sleep with his head on the table. Seal Skin picked him up and carried him to bed (he weighed about a hundred and fifteen pounds), then, too tired to take off her clothes, she climbed in beside him.
===Chinese===
「听着，」他喊道，「我是拉蒙·奥特罗（Ramón Otero），了不起的勇士。我谁也不怕，什么都不怕。全世界随便哪个男人喝酒都喝不过我，凡是两条腿走路的，枪法没一个比我准。只有里科；他是我的朋友。他是了不起的人物，像潘乔·比利亚（Pancho Villa）一样，我深深地爱他。就算他先开枪打我，我也不会打里科。里科是我的朋友，我深深地爱他。」
然后他站起来，打着响指，跳起舞来，鞋跟跺地，扭着胯，笑得海豹皮差点从椅子上摔下去。
快天亮时他趴在桌上睡着了。海豹皮把他抱起来扛到床上（他体重大约一百一十五磅），然后累得连衣服都没脱，爬上床躺在他旁边。

### VII / 七

===Original===
Rico bought all the papers he could find and went up to his room to read them. He sat at his table, his hat tilted over his eyes, with a pair of scissors in his hand, cutting from the papers all the articles dealing with the holdup and the killing of Police Captain Courtney. He arranged the clippings in a neat pile, then read them over and over.
One said:
===Chinese===
里科买齐了他能找到的所有报纸，回自己房间去读。他坐在桌前，帽檐压到眼睛上，手里拿着一把剪刀，把报上所有关于劫案和警察队长考特尼被杀的报道都剪下来。他把剪报码成整整齐齐的一摞，然后翻来覆去地读。
一张报上说：

===Original===
> … the thug who shot Police Captain Courtney was a small, pale foreigner, probably an Italian. He was dressed in a natty overcoat and a light felt hat.
===Chinese===
> ……枪击警察队长考特尼的暴徒是个身材瘦小、面色苍白的异邦人，多半是意大利人。他身穿一件时髦的大衣，戴一顶浅色毡帽。

===Original===
Another:
===Chinese===
另一张：

===Original===
> … Courtney’s murderer was described by one eyewitness as a small, unhealthy-looking foreigner.
===Chinese===
> ……据一名目击者描述，杀害考特尼的凶手是个身材瘦小、一脸病容的异邦人。

===Original===
Rico tore up this clipping.
“Where do they get that unhealthy stuff!” he said. “I never been sick a day in my life.”
===Chinese===
里科把这张剪报撕了。
「什么病容不病容的，他们从哪儿看出来的！」他说。「我这辈子一天病都没生过。」

## Part III / 第三部

### III / 三

===Original===
Sam Vettori’s heavy, dark face looked puffy and his eyes were swollen. He hadn’t been sleeping well lately and he had been drinking whisky. As wine was his usual drink, the whisky indicated a state of mind the reverse of calm. He sat chewing a cold stogie and from time to time pouring himself a shot from the bottle at his elbow. Rico was playing solitaire, his hat tilted over his eyes. The Big Boy sat opposite Vettori, his derby on the side of his head, and his huge fists, fists which at one time had swung a pick in a section gang, lying before him on the table.
The Big Boy shook his head from side to side slowly.
“Not a chance, Sam,” he said, “I can’t do nothing for you. Why, you must be out of your mind. Listen, they’re after me hot and heavy. I got all I can do to take care of number one, see? Things was running too good for you, Sam. That’s your trouble. You thought I was God himself. But listen, I ain’t no miracle man. A stickup more or less, what’s that? But when it comes to plugging a bull like Courtney, that’s out! No, Sam. You’re on your own now. It ain’t gonna be none too healthy for none of us for a while. Just don’t lose your nerve, that’s the main thing. Just hang on and watch the guys that are in the know.”
“You leave that to me,” said Rico without looking up.
“OK,” said the Big Boy. “I think you’re the goods, Rico. But don’t get nervous with that gat of yours, or they’ll put a necktie on you. Get this. No more stickups. No more jobs. Just lay low, all of you. If you run out of jack, I’ll stake you. Now I got to beat it. Don’t call me up no more, Sam. Because I can’t do nothing for you and it might give the bulls an idea.”
The Big Boy got to his feet and stood leaning his huge hairy paws on the table.
===Chinese===
萨姆·维托里那张滞重的黑脸显得浮肿，眼睛也泡肿了。近来他睡不好，一直喝威士忌。他平日喝的是葡萄酒，这威士忌说明他心里远不平静。他坐着嚼一支冷掉的雪茄，不时从肘边的酒瓶里给自己倒上一盅。里科在玩单人纸牌，帽檐压到眼睛上。大人物坐在维托里对面，圆顶礼帽歪戴在头上，两只巨拳摊在面前的桌上——那双手当年在铁路工班里抡过镐头。
大人物缓缓地左右摇头。
「没门，萨姆，」他说，「我帮不了你什么。嗐，你准是疯了。听着，他们正揪着我不放，来势汹汹。我光顾自己都顾不过来，明白吗？你从前日子过得太顺了，萨姆。毛病就出在这儿。你把我当成上帝本尊了。可听着，我不是什么奇迹制造者。多抢一回少抢一回，算个什么？可要说崩了考特尼这样的条子，那就完了！不行，萨姆。从现在起你们只能靠自己了。往后一阵子，咱们谁的日子都不好过。千万别泄了胆气，这是顶要紧的。撑住，盯着那些消息灵通的人。」
「这个你们交给我。」里科说，头也不抬。
「行。」大人物说。「我看你是块好料，里科。可你那杆枪别沉不住气，不然他们会给你套上一条『领带』。记住。不许再抢。不许再干活。全都给我蛰伏。钱花光了，我接济你们。现在我得走了。别再给我打电话，萨姆。因为我帮不了你，而且没准让条子起疑。」
大人物站起来，把两只毛茸茸的大巴掌撑在桌上。

===Original===
“Why, you guys are lucky and don’t know it. Wood’s manager got so goddamn rattled he identified one of the plainclothes men as the guy that did the inside stand. Jesus, but it was rich! Spike Rieger was boiling. Pretty soon he pinned the manager down and the damn dummy said that the guys that did the job were Poles. So they went out and grabbed Steve Gollancz. Steve and his bunch had just tapped a bank and Steve thought they had the goods on him. It was funny as hell!”
The Big Boy put his head back and brayed. Sam Vettori drummed on the table irritably.
